 succeeded in 0ms:
# LLP 3000.011.003.000: Offline-ready application starters

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 web and Expo starters, client examples, acquisition measurement
**Author:** Codex / GPT-6 (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Revised:** 2026-10-14 (EXPOFIX); 2026-10-13 (§9, DX2 wave-10 amendment); 2026-10-12 (§8: wave 9 production onboarding/browser proof/upgrade); 2026-10-12 (§7.6: wave 7 — the README and init summary say what works offline from the schema's synced tables; init names `--port N` as the one flag for both ports; the first read spreads `otherwiseAll` first); 2026-10-11 (§7.5: wave 6 — the README follows the screen and names `dev --port`, `app.json` ids named after the directory, `usePersonaApp` in the persona picker, `...otherwiseAll` in the first read, a backend-first quickstart); 2026-10-11 (§7: small default starters; the chat reference becomes `--template chat`; §7.1: a starter beside a project's own schema; §7.2: init first, schema second — `check` names starter leftovers, `init --adapt`; §7.3: the Expo starter's audit findings; §7.4: wave 4 — adapt keeps `fixtures/`, `snapback/generated/` is never a conflict, a neutral `Home` screen, `test` skips the unmodified starter rows, the advisories before the install)
**Related:** [Problem](./3000.011.003-offline-application-responsibilities.research.md), [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md), [LLP 3000.008.003](./3000.008.003-whole-application-performance.rfc.md)

## 1. Recommendation

Reuse the existing Vite chat reference as one supported production web output,
exposed through the existing init flow. Default init remains schema-only.
Demonstrate cached-route-first navigation, an offline-loadable shell and visible
pending writes that have no predicted row. Use existing client APIs. Add a complete application journey to
the existing starter/witness tests, rather than introduce another state API.

The current Expo starter already shows pending and stale reads. Do not claim
to add those APIs or expand into a multi-bundler framework. Native execution
is a separate actual-application witness in the evidence proposal.

This is an application composition change. Replica recovery and acquisition
semantics remain in their existing implementation and owning LLP.

## 2. Route and write behavior

For a route to a known conversation, read the held membership/conversation
first. Open a locally readable room without first issuing a canonical-DM
creation mutation. When the local answer is incomplete or unavailable, show
the existing read state and allow the online lookup if connected. A horizon
miss is not proof that the room does not exist. Creating a new DM remains an
explicit operation with its current online/eligibility requirements.

Keep `complete` and `fresh` visible through the example's rendering logic.
Do not equate stale with unusable, loading with empty, or mutation settlement
with a freshly observed replica. A navigation that specifically needs the
new authoritative projection can await that observation, but ordinary held
navigation must not become globally dependent on `sync()`.

Bind saved route and intent metadata to both backend identity and principal.
Persist the minimal ID metadata before dispatch when crash recovery requires
it, then observe the SDK receipt; never add a competing outbox or replay loop.
An unknown/evicted receipt remains unknown. Account B cannot see or submit A’s
intent, including through delayed callbacks. Offline sign-out must remain
possible and does not claim A’s pending writes settled or delete its replica.
Test account switch with pending nonpredicting writes and delayed work.
Completed sign-out means local credentials have been durably removed. A stalled
logout request or refresh response cannot gate local invalidation; a late
refresh or store write cannot restore the credential after completion. A
storage failure remains incomplete and retryable. Ordinary client close keeps
its existing session-preservation behavior; remote revocation is best effort.
A rotation already committed by the server must be allowed to return its
successor for best-effort logout, without adopting or persisting that result.

Use the existing pending-write hook and stable intent id when a mutation has
no predicted row. Reopen observes that same write; it does not mint a new id.
Predicted rows keep their existing `pending` indication. A failed write stays
visible and actionable instead of disappearing with a cleared composer.

## 3. Shell and platform behavior

For the supported production web starter, provide a build-known allowlist app-shell cache
for the build's immutable assets and navigation entry. Cache only immutable build assets and a generic nonpersonalized entry; do not
cache arbitrary same-origin responses or navigation HTML. Keep authenticated API
responses, tokens, uploaded media and user data out of that cache; the SDK
retains the data/media it owns. Scope the worker to the application and use a
versioned shell lifecycle so one deployment does not delete a still-running
tab's assets mid-session. Development hot reload should not use a stale
production cache.

If the starter's bundler already provides shell caching, configure that path
rather than install a competing worker. Make the supported output shape
explicit; do not promise arbitrary bundlers automatically acquire offline
reload. Test an actual new page realm with network unavailable.

Expo uses the installed bundle and published native device module. Expo Go's
online fallback remains explicit. No Hermes export is relabeled a durable
native execution result; the evidence proposal owns that execution.

## 4. Residency is a measured application choice

Connect the starter's instructions to the existing
`test --residency --fixture ... --as ...` command and actual client witnesses.
Show which tables supply the screen, which are fully acquired, and which use
a horizon. Reuse current diagnostics rather than add a new residency report
format. Do not infer real client memory from fixture row counts alone.

The paired residency experiment is deferred until two configurations prove
equivalent offline answers. It is not a required implementation deliverable.
For a future Clique measurement, retain two explicit configurations for a paired
experiment only when both meet the same required offline product scope: the
builder's broad public replication and a candidate using justified horizons
or server-only nonessential aggregates. Record transferred rows/bytes, first
useful content, completed durable acquisition, peak memory and offline reopen
on the real web and native clients. Report any reduction in offline history
or prediction separately; it cannot be claimed as an equivalent speedup.

## 5. Acceptance

Extend existing starter and witness scenarios to cover:

- Prime a room, terminate the app/page, stop the backend, and open its route
  with no attempted create mutation before rendering held history.
- Open an unknown room offline and preserve uncertainty instead of creating
  a duplicate or reporting authoritative absence.
- Persist two pending writes across process/page restart, then reconnect and
  verify ordered settlement with the original ids and no duplicate rows.
- Render a pending nonpredicting write, a failed write and a stale retained
  read through the existing card without an application shadow queue.
- Upgrade the web shell with an older tab open; sign out/switch accounts and
  verify the shell cache contains no authenticated response or media bytes.

Success is a fresh builder retaining those properties while replacing the
starter's business screens. Keep generated/example code volume, route
corrections and measured acquisition beside the result. No new push gate or
global cache service is proposed. If a generic shell solution expands into a
framework, limit the first increment to the one published starter and record
the unsupported build shapes.

## 6. Implementation record — 2026-09-22

The selected implementation adds `init --web`, embedding the maintained
`apps/chat` reference; ordinary init remains schema-only. `shell.ts` is one
Vite production plugin for a single generic entry and hashed emitted assets.
The supported base is `/` or an absolute directory such as `/chat/`; routes
use `#room=<id>`. No SSR or arbitrary navigation fallback is promised. The
worker precaches with credentials omitted, waits for older controlled tabs
to close, then retires that scope's previous shell cache.

The room query reads conversation and membership directly, independent of
the inbox's first page. Incomplete offline absence remains uncertain.
`state.ts` stores only backend/principal-bound route and write references;
`Composer.tsx` persists the mutation ID before SDK dispatch and observes it
with `usePendingWrite`. SDK outbox payloads and replay are unchanged. A
server-only `readMarkers` table and `markRead` operation provide a concrete
nonpredicting write. Receipt display remains reachable when room authority
changes; terminal failures and unknown receipts are explicit. References
contain no message body: editable failed drafts after a process restart
remain a product-specific draft-storage choice, not a second replay queue.

Account changes invalidate the prior view synchronously, before async close
or sign-out work. Offline sign-out leaves the account's replica/outbox
intact. The starter explicitly selects the existing browser session store
for guest continuity and documents that page scripts can read that token.
Shell caching never stores it. The public client contract is unchanged; the
sign-out repair below also applies to native hosts using the local client.

Validation added to existing init and React tests covers no-overwrite,
schema-only default, nonprediction, delayed old-account callbacks and saved
write identity. `witness/starter.mjs` drives actual generated production
output in Chromium: subpath hosting, stopped-backend/new-page offline
reopen, two ordered sends plus a nonpredicting write, same-ID reconnect,
revocation failure and unknown receipts, A/B isolation, real guest-session
reopen/sign-out, cache privacy and old-tab shell replacement.

The M5 execution used Node 24.18.0, the freshly built workspace CLI/client/wasm and an actual `init --web` application served under `/chat/` in headless
Chromium. Final results:

- Web initializer: 2/2 new tests passed; default schema-only and no-overwrite
  behavior remain covered.
- Reference schema check and TypeScript check passed.
- Existing React suite: 14/14 passed, including preserved drafts, delayed
  old-account results, saved-ID-before-dispatch and backend isolation.
- Production application witness: passed on attempt 5, exit 0. A fresh page
  with the actual backend stopped recovered the original three pending IDs
  (one nonpredicting marker and two messages). Authoritative reconnect
  sequences were `[2, 3, 4]`; exactly two message rows appeared, in order,
  without duplicates.
- The same run passed A/B isolation, offline sign-out, real guest identity
  across offline page restart, credential removal without replica deletion,
  failed receipts after restart, unknown receipts, shell privacy, a failed
  same-assets worker update, old-tab asset survival and new-build activation.

The first attempts repaired witness setup (a shadowed path helper, hoisted
package resolution/import-only export conditions, and the out-of-scope
observer's empty 404); none is counted as a product pass. The final remote
log is `/tmp/sb4-3011-20260922/starter-browser-5.log`; root retains it with
integration evidence. The application witness uses qualified workspace
packages, not an npm-installed-consumer or native qualification claim.
The emitted worker also passed a small local failure-ownership check:
failed installation preserves an existing cache but removes a new partial
cache. Reference screens, state, schema and build configuration grew from
247 lines / 11,634 bytes to 484 lines / 25,454 bytes; generated
API output is excluded. No new push gate is added.
The acquisition pairing and actual native Clique execution remain in their
separately scoped evidence work.

### Integrated-review repair: local sign-out completion

The independent Astra code review found that the completion screen could
precede credential deletion when logout or refresh stalled. The local client
now fences refresh adoption immediately, including a delayed transport or
response body. It waits for a session-store
write already underway before deleting the credential, so that write cannot
restore a token after completion. Deletion precedes media cleanup; ordinary
close still preserves a successfully rotated session. Remote logout is bounded
best effort and does not gate the local boundary. Storage failure rejects
sign-out for retry; the starter displays progress or that failure, and shows
completed sign-out only after the SDK resolves. The replica/outbox is retained.

The initial repair's final verification on M5 passed: 25 auth regressions, including seven
new persistent-store/transport lifecycle cases; all 49 guide tests; all 15
React cases; package build/typecheck; starter check/typecheck; and the complete
production browser witness (8.429 seconds). The browser held a real logout
request open, observed completed sign-out with no persisted credential, closed
the page, and refused the previous account on a fresh offline page. The first
starter typecheck caught an `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` annotation mismatch;
it was corrected before the final run. Final logs are retained under
`/tmp/sb4-3011-20260922/signout-*-2.log`; the first-run logs remain alongside.
The shared SDK change requires a fresh native Release bundle and repeated
reference witness before final package binding; that result is recorded in
the separate application-evidence report.

The independent Grok follow-up found that aborting a committed rotation could
lose its successor: the retired bearer cannot revoke the server's live family.
The repair keeps the real rotation promise separate from locally cancellable
adoption work. Sign-out captures it before synchronous lifecycle callbacks,
tries the current bearer, and also logs out any returned successor, without
waiting for its response, adopting it, or writing it to storage. The promise
remains tracked through the resolved-but-not-adopted microtask boundary.
The durable deletion ordering and ordinary-close behavior are unchanged.
Focused regressions cover abort-aware and abort-ignoring delayed requests,
delayed body parsing, a forever-stalled rotation, the resolved microtask race,
and real server family revocation after local completion.

Final follow-up verification on M5 passed on `5554e9a24`: 28 auth regressions,
five real-server cases, 49 guide tests, 15 React cases, package and starter
checks/typechecks, and the complete production browser witness (8.856 s).
Logs are retained as `/tmp/sb4-3011-20260922/successor-*.log`.
The maintained native reference was rebuilt in Release, freshly installed on
the owned iPhone 17 Pro / iOS 26.5 simulator, and passed both witness segments
in 23.128 s: offline 8.600 s; process restart 11.993 s. Both original write IDs
survived the restart and settled once, in order. Four extracted video frames
show the pending and sent states. The native framework bytes match runs 1
and 2; the repaired JavaScript is embedded in the newly hashed app bundle.
Source, installed-app and framework manifests plus the summary are retained
as `native-reference-3-*.json`, with receipts, videos and frames under
`native-reference-3/` in the same artifact directory. The owned simulator was
shut down and port 49380 left free. This is qualification of the maintained
reference built from workspace artifacts; installed-package and new Clique
application proof remain separately scoped.


The exact staged 0.2.32 client/platform tarballs subsequently passed the same
production browser journey in 9.468 s, using a fresh npm install and generated
`init --web` output. All package registry hashes match the qualified bytes.
[The publication record](../snapback4/reports/20260922-application-evidence.md#publication-and-installed-package-result)
binds these observations. The actual new Clique application's native exercise
subsequently ran after its independent grading.

The [corrective 0.2.33 qualification](../snapback4/reports/20260922-native-media-repair.md#corrective-release-qualification)
preserves an initial timeout at the final new-worker navigation, with unknown
cause. An observer-only rerun passed all six stages in 11.450 s, then a fresh
uninstrumented control passed in 10.567 s. Tarballs, dependency locks, witness
code, assertions and deadlines were unchanged; no product repair followed
this timeout. The actual Clique [public-package native extension](../snapback4/reports/20260922-clique-native-extension.md#final-public-package-result)
passed all three scenarios on SDK/CLI 0.2.33 and Expo 0.2.24; those results
remain separate from these production-web observations.

## 7. Revision 2026-10-11: small default starters, chat as a template

Wave 1 of the app farm (100 agents, each building a new app on 0.4.16) found
the full chat reference a poor first scaffold: 31 lanes deleted chat routing,
intents, workers and screens before writing their own app, and its files
carried this repository's design history (LLP pointers, "nothing compiles it
until week 2"). The decision:

- `init --web` and `init --expo` write one screen over one table: a todo list
  with guest sign-in, a live `usePage` query and three `useMutation` writes,
  on the same `createApp` + device composition the chat uses. Toolchain pins
  still come from the maintained reference apps, so the two cannot drift.
- The chat reference stays the supported production output of §1 and its
  witnesses, reachable as `init --web --template chat` and
  `init --expo --template chat` (also `new <dir> --template chat --web`).
- Every starter is written without `@ref` pointers: init keeps each
  comment's gloss and drops the pointer into this repository's records.
- The Expo starters ship `expo-env.d.ts` (`/// <reference types="expo/types" />`).
  Without it `process.env` is typed only when some import happens to load
  `expo-modules-core`'s globals, so replacing `src/client.ts` broke the
  starter's own typecheck under TypeScript 6 (16 lanes).
- A starter `package.json` created where no package and no `.js` script
  exists above or below keeps the starter's `"type"` (Vite's ESM); otherwise
  the inherited interpretation rule above still applies.
- `init` still refuses all-or-nothing by default; `--skip-existing` keeps every
  existing file (never overwriting `snapback/*.q`) and writes only the missing
  ones, and `E_INIT_CONFLICT` names that recipe instead of leaving agents to
  init elsewhere and copy over their schema. `--port N` writes the backend URL
  the client calls; the Vite starters pin port 5173 with `strictPort`.

### 7.1 Wave 2 (0.4.17): a starter beside a project's own schema

Wave 2 of the app farm (100 agents on 0.4.17) mostly wrote the backend first
and ran `init --web/--expo --skip-existing` afterwards. The 0.4.17 starter then
wrote its `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and `test.cases.jsonl` beside a schema with no
`todos` table, and the fail-closed fixture loader turned a green project red
(19 lanes). The decisions:

- **Any `snapback/*.q` is the project's schema.** `init` reads them before
  writing. The starter's `schema.q` is a conflict (or, with `--skip-existing`,
  kept out) when another `.q` file exists, and the todo starter's fixtures and
  cases are written only beside the starter's own schema; init says which it
  skipped. A stray `todos.jsonl` that 0.4.17 wrote names the starter in the
  loader's refusal.
- **The screen follows the kept schema's sign-in.** With `use personas` and no
  `use identity`, nobody can sign in as a guest, so the web and Expo starters
  write a persona picker (`openApp(persona)` → `createApp({ url, persona,
  device })`, one app per persona, closed on switch) around the same `Todos`
  screen; a password-only or identity-less schema gets a one-line pointer.
- **Vite's config is `vite.config.mts`** (both the starter and the chat
  template). The package's `"type"` stays whatever the project chose, including
  `npm init -y`'s `"commonjs"`, and Vite still loads the config as ESM, with no
  CommonJS-API warning (17 lanes). This replaces rewriting the package type.
- **Ports move together.** `--web-port N` sets Vite's `server` and `preview`
  ports (strict); without it, `--port N` puts the page on N+1 so a project
  confined to a port range stays inside it. init prints both URLs.
- **Adapting the starter is the expected path.** `package.json` scripts compare
  as commands (option order is not a conflict); a caret range of the starter's
  own `snapback4` version, as `npm i snapback4` saves it, is pinned to the
  exact version rather than refused; `.gitignore` covers `dist/`; the page
  title and Expo app name come from the directory; the starter CSS lives in
  `src/styles.css`. The conflict's first line names `--skip-existing`.

### 7.2 Wave 3 (0.4.18): init first, schema second

Wave 3 of the app farm (100 agents on 0.4.18) showed that §7.1's fixes fire
only when the schema exists *before* `init`. The README and the brief lead
agents to `init --web/--expo` first; they then replace `snapback/schema.q` and
are left with the todo starter's `fixtures/todos.jsonl` and cases (`test` runs
nothing), a guest sign-in screen over a persona-only schema (`auth.guest()`
refuses `E_AUTH`), or, rerunning init to get the picker, `E_INIT_CONFLICT`
(21 lanes, 41 minutes). The decisions, which make the outcome independent of
the order:

- **Leftovers are judged by the starter's bytes.** A starter file is a
  leftover only while it is byte-identical to what init wrote (the screen
  and client compared after normalizing the backend port and the persona
  list, the two values init varies) *and* it no longer fits the schema: the
  fixtures and cases when no `todos` table with `list`/`add`/`toggle`/`remove`
  exists; the screen and client when the schema's sign-in wants the other
  screen (guest for `use identity` with guests, the persona picker for
  `use personas` without `use identity`, or a picker for a different cast).
  A file the project changed is never reported as a leftover and never
  touched. No manifest is written: the starter text embedded in the binary is
  the reference, so a project that has never run 0.4.19's init still qualifies.
- **`check` names them, `init --adapt` fits them.** A successful `check`
  ends with a `starter:` block listing each leftover and its reason, then
  one command, `snapback4 init --adapt` (`check --json`: `starterLeftovers`
  with `files`, `advice` and `fix`). It never changes the exit status.
  `init --adapt [dir]` removes the unmodified fixtures (0.4.19 also removed
  an emptied `fixtures/`; §7.4 reverses that), and rewrites the unmodified screen and client to exactly
  what schema-first init writes for the same schema and port. A password-only
  or identity-less schema, and an adapted screen whose `Todos` component still
  calls the starter's operations, get advice lines rather than a rewrite.
  `--adapt` accepts only `--web`/`--expo` beside it and refuses without a
  `snapback/*.q`.
- **A project's `snapback/*.q` is never a conflict.** With any `.q` present,
  init leaves the starter's `schema.q` out with or without `--skip-existing`,
  lists the project's `.q` files as kept, and never writes through a linked
  schema. This replaces §7.1's "a conflict (or, with `--skip-existing`, kept
  out)". When an `E_INIT_CONFLICT` lists the files of an earlier starter that
  `--adapt` would fit, its first line and its rewrite name `init --adapt`.
- `test`'s refusal of a stray `todos.jsonl` names `init --adapt` as well.

Not done: `test` does not silently skip starter fixtures; a run that
executes nothing stays a refusal, and the command to fix it is one line.
(§7.4 skips the unmodified starter rows loudly instead.)

### 7.3 Wave 3 (0.4.18): the Expo starter's audit findings

Seventeen of the thirty Expo lanes reported `npm install`'s 22 findings
(7 moderate, 15 high) and the deprecated `uuid@7`, and several read
`npm audit`'s "fix" (Expo 44) as advice. A real install of the 0.4.18
starter from the registry resolves three advisories, all under `expo`:

| Advisory | Package (path) | Patched release | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| GHSA-w5hq-g745-h8pq (moderate, 7 findings) | `uuid@7.0.3` (`@expo/config-plugins` → `xcode@3.0.1`) | `uuid` ≥ 11.1.1 | **Overridden**: `"overrides": { "xcode": { "uuid": "11.1.1" } }`. `xcode` calls CommonJS `require('uuid').v4()`, which 11.x still exports (12+ is ESM-only), so the override is scoped to `xcode` and pinned to 11.1.1 |
| GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm (high) | `braces@3.0.3` (`@expo/metro` → `metro-file-map` → `micromatch`) | none: every release is in range | Accepted, documented: Metro's file watcher; nothing reaches it but the project's own globs |
| GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv (high) | `node-forge@1.4.0` (`@expo/cli`, `@expo/code-signing-certificates`) | none: every release is in range | Accepted, documented: the Expo CLI's update code signing, unused by the starter |

`expo_package()` adds the override, so `init --expo`, `--expo --template chat`
and `new --template social` all carry it; an existing `package.json`'s own
`overrides` is kept as written (init only adds absent keys). Measured with a
real install, `npm audit` (and `--omit=dev`, since `expo` is a runtime
dependency) report 15 high and no deprecation; `npm run typecheck` and
`npx expo export -p web` pass. The starter README names both remaining
advisories, why they do not reach the app bundle, and that `audit fix --force`
downgrades to SDK 44.

Re-pinning was measured and not taken: Expo 57.0.27 (the newest SDK 57 patch)
resolves the same `@expo/cli` and `@expo/metro`, so the same 15 findings, and
would also move `expo-sqlite` (~57.0.4), which the native device links; the
reference app `apps/chat-expo` and its witnesses own that pin (§7), and this
change does not move it.

### 7.4 Wave 4 (0.4.19): the starter path after the schema

Wave 4 of the app farm (100 agents on 0.4.19) put 33 lanes (45 minutes) on
the starter path after the schema. One cause was a 0.4.19 regression; the
others were §7.2 stopping short. The decisions:

- **Adapt removes files, never a directory** (17 lanes). 0.4.19's adapt
  removed the two todo fixtures and then the emptied `fixtures/`, while its
  output named only the files; the documented next step, writing
  `fixtures/<table>.jsonl`, failed with `ENOENT` (and `ls fixtures/` after
  adapt explained the "adapt exits 2" reports). Adapt now removes only the
  files it names and says `fixtures/` stays. A project's own fixture file
  beside the starter's is never touched.
- **`snapback/generated/` is never a conflict** (6 lanes). Schema first,
  `check`, then `init --web` refused `E_INIT_CONFLICT` on the
  `snapback/generated/package.json` that `check` had written. Everything under
  `snapback/generated/` is regenerable output: init replaces the file it
  writes there (only `package.json`) and never lists it as a conflict.
- **Script spellings that run the same command are the same command.** A
  leading `npx`, and tsc's default project (`-p`/`--project` naming
  `tsconfig.json`, `./tsconfig.json` or `.`), no longer differ from the
  starter's script; init keeps the project's spelling and says so
  (`kept your package.json scripts.typecheck "tsc --noEmit" (the same
  command as the starter's …)`). A different command, such as
  `tsc --noEmit -p other.json` or another `build`, still refuses: keeping it
  would leave the starter's README instructions running something else.
- **A screen that compiles against any schema** (8 lanes). After §7.2's
  adapt, the screen behind the sign-in still called `api.list`/`add`/
  `toggle`/`remove` and the app did not typecheck. The starter screen is now
  composed of a sign-in head (guest, or persona picker) and a body: the
  todo list, or a neutral `Home` that shows the signed-in viewer
  (`useSignedInViewer()`), the link state and `Object.keys(api)`, with a
  comment naming the hooks and `snapback/generated/api.ts`. The parts are
  whole files whose single-line named imports init merges per module. Both
  orders write the same bytes: schema-first init beside a schema without
  the todo operations writes `Home`, and adapt rewrites an unmodified
  starter screen of either body and either sign-in to the one the schema
  wants (restoring the operations brings `Todos` back). A password-only or
  identity-less schema still gets advice for the sign-in, but its body is
  adapted too, so the app typechecks. Leftover detection recognizes all four
  pairs by bytes; a changed file is never touched. Verified with real
  installs from the registry: web (`npm init -y` first) and Expo, init
  first → schema → `init --adapt` → `check` → typecheck → build, for the
  guest and persona sign-ins.
- **`test` skips the unmodified starter rows, loudly** (4 lanes). A
  `fixtures/todos.jsonl` byte-identical to the starter's, beside a schema
  with no `todos` table, is skipped with `test: WARNING: skipped
  fixtures/todos.jsonl — … snapback4 init --adapt removes it`; the rest of the
  run proceeds. Any other file for a missing table, including a changed
  `todos.jsonl`, still refuses the run. An unmodified starter
  `test.cases.jsonl` still fails on its first unknown operation, now with a
  hint naming `init --adapt`, so a project that has written nothing of its
  own does not pass vacuously.
- **The advisories before the install** (15 lanes). Neither remaining
  advisory has a patched release as of 2026-10-11 (`braces` ≤ 3.0.3 and
  `node-forge` ≤ 1.4.0 are the whole vulnerable range; 3.0.3 and 1.4.0 are
  the latest releases), so no further `overrides` is possible. `init --expo`
  now prints, before `npm install` runs, that the install takes minutes and
  reports about 15 expected high advisories and not to run
  `npm audit fix --force`; the starter README opens with the same notice
  above its first command, and the package README's Expo row carries it.

Not done: `init --expo` still writes the todo starter's `slug`, `scheme`
and bundle identifiers (067, 035, 038), and `check`'s `starter:` block still
lists reasons rather than a one-line plan; both are smaller than the items
above and stay open.

### 7.5 Wave 6 (0.4.21): the README, the app ids and the persona lifecycle

Eleven lanes of the 0.4.21 farm (29 minutes; analysis `wave6/analysis/patterns.md`
#12) still met the starter's todo identity after their own schema took over.

- **The README follows the screen** (042, 094). Adapt rewrote the screen
  and client but left a README describing the todos table, guest sign-in and
  plain `snapback4 dev` beside a client on another port. The starter README
  is now chosen with the screen: the Todos README with `Todos`, and with
  `Home` a README titled after the directory (`# Bake Sale`) that describes
  `snapback/*.q`, the sign-in the schema gets (guest, or the persona picker
  and its `serve` limit), `fixtures/<table>.jsonl`, the Home screen and its
  hooks; the Expo one keeps the Todos README's advisory box, toolchain,
  audit and native-proof notes word for word (sliced from it, so they cannot
  drift). Schema-first init writes it; adapt rewrites an unmodified starter
  README (byte-equal to either README at the ports it names) to the one the
  schema wants, in both directions, beside a changed screen too (the README
  itself is unmodified; the changed screen is still never touched). A
  README's `npx snapback4 dev` names `--port N` when init had one.
- **`app.json` ids are the directory's** (067; open since §7.4). `init
  --expo` names the slug, URL scheme and iOS/Android ids after the directory
  (`bake-sale`, `bakesale`, `dev.snapback4.bakesale`; a name starting with a
  digit gets `app` in front, `4h-club` → `app4hclub`). For projects started
  by 0.4.21 or earlier, `check` names an `app.json` whose four values are
  still the todo starter's and adapt replaces exactly those strings; a
  project that changed any of them keeps its file.
- **One hook owns a persona's app** (034 055 057, and 071's
  setState-during-render; LLP 3000.013 §6.7). Both persona pickers call
  `usePersonaApp(persona, openApp)` instead of a hand-written effect.
- **The first read names its fallback in one token.** `FirstRead` spreads
  `...otherwiseAll(other => readPresentation(other).why?.message ?? "…")`
  (LLP 3000.031 §8), so the refusal still shows.
- **Backend first in the README** (022, 100). The package README's Start
  here opens with four commands — plain `init` (only `snapback/schema.q`),
  `check`, `test --fixture fixtures`, then `init --web`/`--expo` — so a
  schema can pass its tests before any UI exists and the starter written
  last fits it with nothing to adapt (319 of 320 budgeted lines).

Verified with real installs from the local registry (0.4.21's dependencies,
with this tree's `snapback4` package installed over them): web and Expo,
init first → schema → `init --adapt` → `check` → typecheck → build.
Not done: `init --personas a,b,c` (one lane); `check --fix` removing an
unmodified `todos.jsonl` without adapt (072; adapt already does it, and
`check` names the command).

### 7.6 Wave 7 (0.4.22): what works offline, and one flag for both ports

- **The README's offline sentence follows the schema (R3, 5 React lanes:
  004 038 064 082 091; three named it first).** 0.4.22 made the README
  describe the project's schema but kept the todo starter's "It works
  offline and syncs when the link returns", so an all-`online only` app's
  README (and init's "offline-capable" summary) promised offline reads it
  does not have (064: "the only observed misleading behavior"; 091: "a trust
  issue"). The Home README now carries one paragraph, starting "Offline", chosen
  from the compiled schema: no synced table ("reads need the server; writes
  made offline are kept on this device and sent when the link returns"),
  every table synced, or the synced tables by name; a schema that does not
  compile yet gets the general rule (tables that declare `sync` work
  offline). init's summary says the same in a clause. `init --adapt` (and
  `check`'s leftover report) recognizes an unmodified starter README whatever
  its offline paragraph says, including 0.4.22's sentence, and rewrites only
  that paragraph when the schema's synced tables changed; a schema that does
  not compile right now never triggers that rewrite. The todo starter's own
  README keeps its claim: its one table syncs. The Expo opening no longer
  says device builds "work offline" unconditionally either.
- **`--port N` is the one flag (11 lanes; 027 054 068 079 091 asked for
  it).** `init --web --port N` already wrote the client URL, the README's
  `dev --port N` and the Vite dev and preview port N+1, but init's line named
  only `--web-port`. Without `--port`, the line now says "`init --web --port
  N` sets the backend to N and the page to N+1 together" (Expo: the client
  URL and README together), and `init --help` describes `--port` that way.
- **The first read spreads `...otherwiseAll` first** (LLP 3000.031 §8: the
  spread is order-free now; first is where a copied handler is read as an
  override).

## 8. Wave 9: production onboarding and a browser proof

**Implementer:** Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-12 (DX workstream).
The commission covers wave 9 fix list #6, #7, #10, #13, #14 and the jobs
recipe in #18. REL owns the persona-to-password deploy checklist; HAND owns
protocol negotiation and hot-activation envelope consistency.

Password identity produces signup/login forms on web and Expo, retaining the
existing session-store custody. Schema-first init and unmodified-screen adapt
select the password form; edited screens remain application-owned. Both
starters honor runtime `?api=` on web. Expo chooses the existing device-less
journal on web when no table syncs, preserving nativeDevice on native builds.
Expo `build:web` clears Metro on every export so a changed public URL is
compiled; Vite builds again from its current environment.

`check` emits advisory N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER for application tables lacking
an insert/upsert operation or blocked by positive bootstrap dependencies.
Insert rules and read-through parents are evaluated over a least fixed point
of potentially populated tables; jobs write as system, service-seat branches
are potential roots. Negated and unknown comparisons remain potentially
satisfiable: this is a sound conservative warning for the covered positive
fragment, not a general satisfiability proof. Native jobs are opaque and suppress
this absence analysis. A warning points to `guide deploy --section first admin`.
No rule, planner, admission or outbox changes are made. Fixture argument drift
is checked without execution with the existing wire decoder and argument
validator; saved-result references remain unknown. Neither note fails check.

Wave 10's root grouping, persona identity summary and intentional-input fixture
scoping are refined by [LLP 3000.036.002](./3000.036.002-intent-aware-lints-and-bootstrap.rfc.md);
its service-grant change belongs to the rule-language work, while DX2 owns
new starter, upgrade and witness recipes.

`snapback4 witness web [dist]` serves an existing export on an allocated
loopback port, launches headless Chromium (SNAPBACK4_CHROMIUM overrides cache
and standard-path discovery), opens the real page with `?api=&witness=web`,
then uses its persona or sign-in UI (signup on the owned empty backend). It calls that app's authenticated SDK
client, using the first generated durable mutation or `--mutation` and `--args`, and
requires terminal `sent`; failed/pending/timeout are nonzero with a reason.
The app registers its client and generated API with
`registerWebWitness(app, api)` from `snapback4/witness-web`; starters include
this hook. It exists only under the explicit query flag, adds no authority,
and is callable by the page's own scripts already holding that client.
This proves exported client/auth/write integration, not the application's
composer UI or an arbitrary user journey. Missing registration refuses.
A fresh browser and an owned `dev --memory --port 0 --no-watch` make default
runs repeatable without changing the project's durable store. `--url` never
resets a caller-owned server; a deterministic id derived from principal,
operation selection and canonical arguments deduplicates repeated proof
writes under that store's receipt guarantees. Different arguments are new
intents. Credentials are supplied through a named environment variable,
never printed. Cleanup records the owned backend immediately at spawn, even before readiness;
its supervisor PID also stops it if the witness dies. It closes the browser/static
server and only the owned backend; long polls never require networkidle.

`upgrade [version]` detects npm/bun/pnpm by packageManager then lockfile,
refusing ambiguous locks. It resolves latest once, preflights core and installed
Expo adapter versions, installs that same exact pin through the manager,
preserves dependency sections and verifies resulting package pins. Native
rebuild and check/acceptance remain explicit. Package-manager failures return
nonzero; its ordinary lockfile/install recovery applies.

The wire table distinguishes raw responses from SDK Read/Write states.
Dropping a null mutation id is a serialization amendment only: a request
without id remains a fresh, non-deduplicated write. Callers mint ids for retries.
No id is fabricated after execution. Omitted ids remain omitted on error paths.

### Wave 9 review dispositions

Round 1 Muse: NOT READY, four material findings. All accepted: wrap the
pre-execution error response in null-id omission; distinguish guest/password
in adaptation state and reporting; retain password README text for Todos/Home
through adaptation; use pnpm's own save-exact/save-dev/save-optional flags.
The first fresh Sol review and the corrected revision's reviews follow under
`llp/reviews/`. Existing card budgets stay unchanged; the photo details now
link to the published client reference beside the new password recipe.

Round 1 fresh Sol: NOT READY, two material findings. Accepted: Playwright
credential-entry failures now produce a fixed diagnostic; arbitrary foreign
exception logs are withheld and known password text is removed from owned
diagnostics. A disabled-input Chromium case checks both output streams.
The wire table now includes raw loading/null-placeholder replies, and the
client's empty-list sentence names cuts, continuations and pending predictions.
Sol also verified startup SIGTERM cleanup and requested an immutable final review.

The first fixed-commit Sol review found that untouched 0.4.24 clients no longer
matched after the URL/registration additions. Accepted: recognize the exact
previous client templates (including their port/persona/journal variants),
never arbitrary stripped application source. A base-template regression covers
web/Expo, guest/persona, migration to password and edited-client protection.
The witness selects durable mutations only; an ephemeral signal cannot prove
repeatable durable write custody. Published guides use user-facing deployment
pointers; workstream ownership remains here and in the workstream report.

Round 2 Muse independently found the same previous-template gap and the
non-durable signal issue; both are fixed by 282a676c79. It pinned its findings
to the requested 8f8c389d14 even though its response arrived after the repair.
Round 3: both [Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave9-dx.round3.muse.md)
and [fresh Sol](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave9-dx.round3.sol.md) are READY
on the same immutable 282a676c79 revision, with no material findings.
The remaining persona-picker minor is corrected: when an export does not
honor `?as=`, the witness clicks the named persona button and verifies the
requested `dev:` viewer before admission. A real browser case emulates Expo's
picker. Generated no-sync Expo comments now describe the journal accurately.

Round 4 checks that bounded picker delta independently: both
[Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave9-dx.round4.muse.md) and
[fresh Sol](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave9-dx.round4.sol.md) are READY
on immutable 6e0cbbb088, with no material findings. Their shared minor test
suggestion is incorporated: assert the picker emulation actually changes the
export source, so a future template edit cannot silently bypass its coverage.
Final affected Rust proof: 373 passed; browser/Expo/upgrade file: 11 passed;
README/API/browser combined: 22 passed; docs budget and ref-check pass.
No review, capability handshake, planner change or outbox change is deferred.


## 9. Wave 10: upgrade deltas and visible form proof

**Implementer:** Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-13, DX2 commission.
Status of this amendment: Implemented; both commissioned blind families are READY on the final revisions recorded below.

A check remembers its compiler version, schema source identity, emitted API,
note counts by code and refusal counts in `.snapback4/check-surface.json`.
The first check using a different version compares that baseline with the same
project: newly added notes by code, changed generated row/operation types, and
new compiler refusals. If the source changed too, the delta says so; it does not
attribute application edits to the compiler. Missing baseline means explicitly
unavailable comparison. Compiler refusals preserve the last baseline and generated
API, so a refusal cannot erase the useful pre-upgrade record. Normal same-version
checks refresh it after successful check. A server/native qualification failure
can leave regenerated, valid Quarry types while retaining the previous baseline. JSON check output stays one
JSON object; human delta goes to stderr in that mode. Snapshot write failures
are reported and never invalidate valid product types. No store adoption, sync
or authority change is introduced.

Upgrade takes the old installed CLI's check snapshot before installation, then
invokes the newly installed CLI's check after pinning. Compiler path resolution runs in a fresh Node process
for each check: package-manager symlink replacements (pnpm) cannot reuse the
helper's cached old realpath after installation. It preserves the package
manager's dependency sections and ordinary recovery. A compiler refusal after a
successful install returns nonzero with a message distinguishing installation
from qualification; it never rolls back the packages silently. Module-only backends are projects too: all `snapback/*.q` files qualify through
the same loader; schema.q is not required. Without any Quarry source, upgrade
reports package pins only. Exact apps receive one rebuild pointer:
`bun exact.mjs snapback --rebuild` (HS owns the command and device handshake).
`guide changes` packages the release card; its version is bound to the compiler
version and its factual nullable-count examples are covered by the guide tests.
Nullable numeric aggregate writes offer `EXPR ?? 0` as an authored choice, not
auto-apply: hidden keys remain null, authorized missing count groups return zero.

The witness uses the declared persona independently of a guest-only sign-in
screen by selecting createApp's normal persona option under the explicit
witness flag. It still verifies the actual viewer before writing. Custom apps
retain their visible persona picker; a missing persona app/picker refuses. No persona
works against serve. Default starter examples use guest sign-in; `--as alice`
on a starter that declares alice also works.

A visible form proof is opt-in with `--field LABEL --value TEXT --button LABEL`.
It fills the labelled control, presses the labelled button and observes that
click's matching durable mutation through the registered app client. After filling it arms the exact labelled button, and accepts a mutation entered while
that button's trusted Chromium click remains active (through either client or app).
A form submit event is not click evidence. The default starter calls its shared
submit command from onClick (preventing the duplicate default form action); Enter
continues to use that same command. Short microtasks that remain inside the active dispatch may submit; work after
dispatch ends may not. Finish longer asynchronous validation before enabling
submit. A private witness-only options symbol follows unapproved App calls
through readiness, so a submit/other-context continuation cannot borrow the
restored enclosing click at Client entry. It is never serialized or a client
card field. Tagging reads each supported option once and retains both values (id and
waitSeconds), including inherited/non-enumerable getters, without changing the
caller object. This scoped proof never attributes background
or input autosave writes to a button. Before clicking it arms a fresh observer: an existing sent status or row cannot satisfy
this proof. It waits for terminal sent and asserts the configured args when
provided; failed or timed-out custody is nonzero. No second SDK mutation is
injected. The ordinary SDK-only witness remains available without these flags.
React Native web uses keyboard events (`pressSequentially`) to settle state;
buttons use their accessible labels, not testID. Registration is flag-gated and
cleanup restores its instrumentation. Starters label their field, render write
status above the list, and supply a favicon.

Port selection precedence is explicit --port/--web-port, explicit --port-range,
then the complete PORT_LO/PORT_HI environment pair, then existing defaults.
Ranges are validated before writing any files, and every selected port lies in
them. The client URL, backend npm launcher, frontend dev/preview launcher,
README and witness script share those selections. Later runtime URL overrides
remain supported; the init range is a scaffold input, not an authority rule.
HS owns the Vite Node declarations; DX2 adds no parallel type shim.

Expo's web client and README derive their device-versus-journal choice from the
same compiled sync detection. Its smoke helper uses the schema's password,
guest or persona sign-in (password only on prime, with a witness-supplied test
credential; it never puts a secret in source). The helper is inert in ordinary
launches and other segments and waits for restoration. The native device and
normal identity custody remain unchanged.

Ordinary durable React `run()` owns an onWrite observer before admission and
waits for sent/failed, independently of read subscribers and mounted effects.
Early settlement cannot be missed and concurrent calls match only their own
saved/minted intent id. The promise releases its observer on terminal response
or thrown admission. Offline it waits while the client lives; closing the client
ends its ability to collect a receipt, so callers keep the App alive until
settlement or use the explicit custody option and saved id. Existing
`{ waitSeconds: 0 }` returns pending custody immediately; no pending result is
relabelled sent. This amends the default hook boundary, not client.mutate,
outbox durability, authority or replay-result availability. `sentResult` and
`otherwiseAll` already exist; the recipe and tests cover null, replay and all
five read meanings. No client card word is added.

Wire id omission already landed in §8 and is re-tested with the documented
full envelopes. Guides add tested loop writes and projection identity, list
supported formats (handle and url; email remains application validation), spell
out fixed-period UTC epoch anchoring and periodEnd, and carry the asset upload
route. Aliases resolve optional param, mutation default and service seat.
Non-TTY reads print a complete card; explicit section/find still narrows it.
REL2 owns release lifecycle narration, DIAG owns general diagnostics and PAGE2
owns planner/rules language; DX2's nullable-write guidance changes no semantics.


Wave-10 review record: round 1 Muse READY; fresh Sol NOT READY with four
material counterexamples. All accepted: preserve complete wave-9 starter byte
variants in adapt, recognize module-only projects, bind form admission to the
actual labelled button's active click, and refuse frontend overflow before
writing. Add controls for unrelated background writes and highest-port ranges.
The no-sync Todos README and stale piped-output wording are corrected as well.


Round 2: Muse READY; fresh Sol identified a later requestSubmit inheriting the
saved click as a material false positive. Accepted: the bridge now recognizes
only entry in the trusted clicked button event, never standalone form submit.
The starter's shared submit command is entered from onClick and prevents the
duplicate browser action. A Chromium requestSubmit-after-broken-click control
refuses. Restored independent section/find and executable recipe tests removed
by the earlier piped-output test edit. No product behavior beyond this scoped
proof boundary changes in the final delta.


Round 3 found that Chromium can retain window.event through short microtasks,
and that App readiness could restore a containing click after an unapproved
submit call. The active-dispatch contract now states the first behavior honestly;
private witness-only provenance on App calls closes the second without changing
write arguments, intent ids, wire or outbox. Add same-dispatch microtask success,
synchronous requestSubmit refusal and script-click controls. Round 3 Sol also
reported accidentally seeing review-disposition prose; retain that limitation
and use a fresh, explicitly bounded behavior excerpt for the final review.


Round 4: Muse READY; fresh Sol found that spread alone drops inherited and
non-enumerable supported options. Accepted: read/preserve id and waitSeconds
explicitly when adding witness provenance, including class/private getters and
frozen objects. Approved calls retain the original options reference. Regression
controls verify saved retry identity, immediate custody and caller immutability.


Round 5 adds the enumerable/stateful getter control: destructuring reads id and
waitSeconds once, and the rest excludes them before provenance is added. This
preserves custody/retry values instead of evaluating getters twice. Both ordinary
non-enumerable/private getters and stateful enumerable getters are covered.


Final record — 2026-10-13: both
[Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave10-dx2.round6.muse.md) and
[fresh Sol](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave10-dx2.round6.sol.md) are READY
on the final witness revision 1e1494a46f. A final physical pnpm-style symlink
replacement exposed the running Node resolver's old-package cache; fresh-process
compiler resolution and its integration proof are included in ee18ff20b6.
Both [Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave10-dx2.round7.muse.md) and
[fresh Sol](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-wave10-dx2.round7.sol.md) are READY on
that same final revision, with no material findings. All actual reviews and
briefs are preserved verbatim. No workstream implementation or review is deferred;
physical native/publication qualification is not claimed by these scoped checks.


## 10. Expo web-export qualification — 2026-10-14 (EXPOFIX)

Published 0.4.26's Expo starters configured an SVG favicon. Expo's image
pipeline rejects that input during `expo export -p web`, although the
starter-content tests passed. Remove the optional favicon key and unused SVG
from every Expo starter (Todos, chat and social); keep the SVG in Vite's
public directory, where the browser consumes it directly. An application can
supply its own raster favicon. `init --adapt` removes only the exact generated
SVG and its exact setting, preserving custom images, paths and other config.
Fresh UI starters describe the known Todos schema's offline behavior immediately;
`check` must issue no adaptation advice on any untouched starter.

The publication's installed-consumer proof now installs packed CLI/client and
Expo packages, generates the real web and Expo starters, installs their pinned
toolchain dependencies, runs `check` and typecheck, builds Vite, and runs both
`expo export -p web` and the starter's `build:web`. It also adapts each to an
online-only Home schema and repeats the builds, then installs/typechecks/builds
the web-chat, Expo-chat and social templates. Proofs have a twenty-minute
per-distribution guard; individual commands have five minutes. Expo workers
are bounded at two in the direct export and generated build:web scripts;
unbounded worker pools made the namespace-free proof exceed its command guard. Dependency installation can access
the npm registry with prefer-offline caching; Expo's network checks and
telemetry are disabled. This web proof does not qualify native iOS binaries.

`--prepare-client` also packs Expo as a proof input. Linux qualification requires
`--expo <prepared-expo.tgz>` and binds its SRI plus web/Expo/adapt success in
both Ubuntu and Alpine proof records. The publisher checks that SRI against
its preparation and retains those exact Expo bytes in its stage. Darwin-only
publication and resume run the same starter proof. Expo's native release
publication remains the existing separate native qualification path; this
change does not publish an unqualified native package.

The audit covers all emitted assets/config: Expo's app ids, plugins, entrypoint,
Babel/Metro configs, wasm transformer and dependency versions, Vite's module
config, HTML, public SVG and generated offline shell. No other emitted Expo
image key exists. The changed-schema builds exercise journal/device selection,
generated API and adapted Home/sign-in/README paths. Chat/social receive their own
real installs and exports: social has a Router entrypoint and extra plugins.
The audit found its floating Router range selecting an unqualified release
with an unavailable dependency during install; pin Router to 57.0.23, the
version in the existing workspace lock, and qualify it through the same path.


Historical publication stages without a retained Expo proof input are refused
with a specific explanation, preserving all artifacts. A new version closes
the gap; a pre-gap suite receipt cannot authorize publication without the new
real-toolchain evidence. Format-2 stage layout remains readable when it carries
the required Expo bytes; tests distinguish that from genuine old stages.


The command guard uses the existing live command-owner keeper. Only that
keeper signals its own process group; completion and timeout close its private
pipe, and caller death does the same. Commands are asynchronous so npm's
signal handling cannot block the watchdog. Success requires the command's
zero exit plus two empty group-existence observations; lingering members or
inspection errors refuse. Clean userspaces need no host /proc mount. Cleanup
allows up to five seconds beyond the command guard; deliberately detached
processes are outside group custody, as in the existing suite contract.


EXPOFIX final review record: both
[Muse](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-expofix.round4.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.011.003.000-expofix.round4.sol.md) are READY on
74d144a326, including the process-group custody change. Earlier actual reviews,
the capacity-failed session and all briefs remain verbatim under reviews/.
The first Codex round's unbounded-worker/timeout counterexample produced the
worker cap and live-keeper repair; historical-stage behavior is now explicit.
No full publication receipt, native qualification or upload is claimed here.
# LLP 3000.011.005.000: Qualify the built application on its actual hosts

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Accepted
**Systems:** Snapback 4 application witnesses, Expo Release builds, deployments, provider effects, benchmark records
**Author:** Codex / GPT-6 (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Revised:** 2026-09-29 (served-generation and kept-fact binding for generic native smoke; real-provider exercise remains conditional and unrun)
**Related:** [Problem](./3000.011.005-native-and-production-evidence.research.md), [LLP 3000.009](./3000.009-general-purpose-backend.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.008.003](./3000.008.003-whole-application-performance.rfc.md)

## 1. Recommendation

Use the existing published witness on the application the builder actually
produced, with its exact installed packages. Add the missing application
assertions and run them on an installed native Release build. Then perform
scoped deployment/restore and provider exercises using existing tools.
Record each boundary independently in the existing benchmark run directory.

This proposal adds execution and application integration rather than a second
harness, registry, certification badge or universal publication gate. A
successful native run does not automatically qualify deployment or providers.

## 2. Native application witness

Build and install the Expo app with the actual published native module on a
dedicated compatible simulator. Use the existing `snapback4-expo/witness`
helper and `snapback4 witness ios`; a Hermes export and the Expo Go online
fallback do not satisfy this step. Keep the real screen visible so receipts
and recorded UI refer to the same application behavior.

Prime authorized history, search and viewed ordinary media online. Terminate
the app, stop the backend and verify network failure. Cold-launch and assert
held history/search/media, incomplete horizons where applicable, and no
durable replay of consumed view-once media. Queue two messages and persist
their ids; terminate and relaunch offline, then verify those same pending
intents survived. Reconnect, observe ordered authoritative settlement and
receipt progression without duplicate rows. Learn a membership revocation,
relaunch offline and verify its retained-history/new-delivery boundary.

Use the driver's existing failure receipts, video, logs and run manifest.
The manifest must identify the installed app, native package, backend package,
simulator runtime, origin and exact artifacts. Missing artifacts remain
missing, and retries receive fresh directories. A simulator result is still
not a physical-device, Android or power-loss result.

### Generic smoke, 2026-09-24

`witness ios --smoke` adds a no-app-code first rung to the existing driver.
On an explicit smoke launch the Expo device attaches to the application's
own local client (including `createApp`/`AppProvider`), installs the native
JS error handler and reads counts and sync identity from the kept device.
Ordinary launches do not load the runner. It does not sign in for the app.
Prime waits for online acquisition; a cold offline launch checks persisted
counts and all optional-argument queries from the kept generated program
metadata; restart requires online sync through the exact driver barrier. No query rows enter the
receipts. Screenshots/video still show the real app's visible content.

After backend readiness, the driver reads the committed compiler generation
and binds it to the owned host's reported generation/store identity. It sends
that generation and the complete eligible query facts in the launch payload.
Before any query, the helper requires every runs/held fact to agree with its
kept backend and both device/backend generations to match the launch. The
driver refuses generation/fact changes across restart and checks the receipt
generation; a separate compilation of live source cannot supply these facts. Each smoke receipt records those facts and each
executed query's classification and actual result. Missing facts or a different
eligible operation set fail; classification is never inferred from `loading`.
Device-answerable queries (`runs: device`, including held reads) require data
or an explained refusal offline. Loading, a throw or an uncaught error fails.
Compiler-declared server-only reads (`runs: server`, including ephemeral and
online-only tables) are recorded offline as `online-only: unavailable offline
(expected)`, with their actual `loading` or `E_OFFLINE`-style refusal. This is
expected unavailability, not an answer. Those same eligible server-only queries
must answer in both online prime and restart. Required-argument queries remain
outside generic smoke. Optional-argument native queries are also refused by
generic smoke: their compiler server bit describes conditional module residency,
not unconditional server-only behavior. Use an application witness for those
queries. The native client must still acquire and reopen its
partition; an online-only client fallback cannot satisfy smoke.


A quiet backend's commit watermark cannot advance from reads alone. While
its owned backend and app are stopped, the driver records one zero-write
commit, with no application row, schema, log event or outbox change. The
manifest names this sync marker and all three segment receipts, timings and
screenshots. This is lifecycle evidence, not a substitute for the deeper
application assertions above; empty partitions and required-argument queries
remain explicitly limited evidence.

Round-one review hardening binds restart to the barrier sequence in both the
launch configuration and driver validation, so a pre-barrier tail cannot
stand in for reconnect progress. A successful segment's owned PID must still
exist at deliberate termination. Device state is reread after the render
pause before attesting counts and identity. `createApp` notifies the native
host only after publishing a client; the runner follows replacements and
cancels a closed client's in-flight sample without a failure receipt.

## 3. Acquisition on the same app

Run the full retained fixture on the actual client, identifying the offline
content required before measuring. Record first useful render, completed
durable acquisition, transferred rows/bytes, disk size and peak memory where
the host exposes it, then prove offline reopen. If peak memory cannot be
measured, name the absent metric; do not substitute JavaScript heap for all
native or wasm memory.

Preserve LLP 3000's physical-floor comparison only for equivalent fixture,
host and persistence semantics. Smaller reference-app timings and server
query latencies do not substitute for this measurement. These observations
also evaluate the residency proposal, without making it responsible for
native module correctness.

## 4. Deployment, restore and provider boundaries

Use an isolated disposable deployment with the existing release commands.
Exercise password session recovery, an asset read, schema adoption, a queued
job across process restart and the documented stopped backup/restore path.
Keep restored workers and old production clients fenced; do not reconnect a
prior outbox and claim recovery merely because fresh reads work. Test rollback
as source activation, not database rewind. Record the storage mode and the
precise crash class; this is not physical-power-loss qualification.

For one real push-provider adapter, select a test account/device and explicit
recipient before sending. The user-authorized implementation, publication
and benchmark program does not by itself identify a provider account or a
message recipient. Prepare the adapter and test-double crash cases first;
execute actual external delivery only with that account/recipient authorization.
No secret values belong in artifacts or LLPs.

Distinguish effect admission, provider acceptance and device receipt. Inject
the acceptance-before-local-settlement failure using a labeled test double;
then verify the real provider's supported deduplication/outcome behavior.
If a provider cannot deduplicate or resolve an uncertain result, record the
remaining ambiguity instead of claiming exactly-once external delivery.

## 5. Benchmark integration and availability

Before launching the next comparable Clique run, record package pins, builder
and grader identities, required simulator availability and the committed
probe set, or explicitly declare any protocol deviation. Preserve the frozen
instrument and perform native qualification as an explicit extension after
the cold build and independent grading. The prior builder prompt expressly
made simulator proof optional. Do not change that timed task silently. Do not quietly count a new
native proof as though previous runs used the same environment.

Append a compact coverage table to the existing report: scenario, host,
artifact link, result and limitation. A missing credential, simulator or
signing identity is an execution prerequisite, not a passing product test.
Continue independent browser/server work while that cell remains unavailable;
do not manufacture its outcome or replace it with a compilation check.

## 6. Completion and limits

The native increment is complete when the actual application passes its
installed Release witness and every required artifact is retained. Deployment
and provider increments complete separately on their stated environments.
Any discovered defect receives a reproduction, an affected fix and a rerun
of that boundary before the claim changes.

Run slow host exercises outside the ordinary edit/push loop and on a machine
whose simulator use will not disrupt the user. Use the existing fleet and
owned-process rules. No full battery at an ever-moving main is required.
Do not spend time building more evidence infrastructure when an existing
witness can execute the missing scenario directly.

## 7. Implementation and evidence checkpoint

The bounded operational increment is implemented in the existing
`packages/snapback4/test/release-activation.test.mjs` and `worker.test.mjs`,
without a new harness or provider framework. On the isolated M5 checkout,
the two affected deployment tests and two provider-double tests passed.
Password login/rotation survives the stopped full-deployment copy; the copied
asset, fresh process device, schema adoption and source-only rollback pass.
A genuinely queued scheduled job survives an owned serving process's SIGKILL
before its first chunk and resumes under the same ID exactly once.

The loopback provider double accepts before an owned worker is killed, before
either step or effect settlement. The successor's stable-key reconciliation
produces two submissions and one accepted delivery when the double supports
it. When the outcome is unavailable, it parks without a second submission.
Both paths refuse stale claims. These are process-crash/stopped-copy tests on
SQLite WAL with `synchronous=NORMAL`, not power-loss, real-provider or device
delivery evidence. Old production clients/outboxes and restored effect
workers remain disconnected by the exercise's explicit isolation procedure.

The [execution record and native adaptation plan](../snapback4/reports/20260922-application-evidence.md)
retain commands, timings, artifact locations and coverage limits. A reference
Release witness passed both offline and kill/relaunch segments against the
rebuilt workspace artifact before and after the sign-out review fix; it remains distinct
from the required actual application and an installed npm package. Cal16's frozen
instrument and cold builder packet were not modified. Its cold build and
independent grading are now complete: 104 assertions passed, zero failed and
15 remain unrun; the independent holistic score is 88. The separate extension
uses a preserved copy of the completed app and final M8 fixture, the existing
helper, real app screens and exact published pins. Actual-app installation,
full retained acquisition and the real-provider cell were still pending at
that checkpoint. The candidate native result below advances that evidence;
final public pins and the real-provider cell remain distinct prerequisites.

## 8. Actual-app qualification exposed a native media defect

The [post-grade native failure](../snapback4/reports/20260922-native-media-repair.md)
reached the installed Expo 57 Release app using the published 0.2.32 client
and 0.2.23 native package. After ordinary native acquisition, a successful
HTTP 200 image response produced a 2,062-byte React Native Blob without
`arrayBuffer()`. The ordinary cache calls that method unconditionally, so
the actual image/video hooks refused and no ordinary bytes reached the cache.
The failed `restart-3` attempt remains evidence against the original package;
earlier signing/entitlement setup failures are recorded separately.

The selected remedy stays in the shared asset implementation: use the host's
public `FileReader.readAsArrayBuffer` only when Blob lacks its direct method,
and explicitly own/release response Blobs through the host's available APIs.
Keep once responses outside durable byte extraction, retain caller ownership
of upload Blobs, and preserve browser URL behavior. Native handle release
does not promise instantaneous memory zeroization or removal of decoder copies.
No app shim, native ABI change, new dependency or resource ceiling is needed.
Astra xhigh implements the repair; both requested reviewer families and exact
installed-package native reruns must precede a success claim. The original
cal16 app, grade and score remain frozen across the corrective release.


The first candidate passed ordinary native rendering, then exposed a second
host incompatibility: Expo 57's object-URL polyfill threw while its expected
bridgeless native proxy was absent. A separate ordinary-Blob probe reproduced
the exception with the link online and without consuming another once grant.
The selected transient fallback is public `FileReader.readAsDataURL` when
object-URL creation fails. It copies response bytes into an in-memory base64
URI and never writes them to media storage or refetches the asset. Browser
object URLs remain preferred. Copied data strings cannot be revoked, so the
caller must drop them on screen close; SDK cleanup releases its owned
references and native Blob, not arbitrary copies. This documented tradeoff
preserves the server's single grant and the no-durable-once-storage contract;
it adds neither a private native dependency nor a native ABI. Review and
qualification must check once-file/manifest absence while the actual image is
open as well as after close and process death. The [repair record](../snapback4/reports/20260922-native-media-repair.md)
separates both failures, patches and pending qualification.


Once rendering then passed, including live-handle durable-storage exclusion,
but the actual video player exposed a separate native-cache contract defect.
The valid original MP4 and cached file were byte-identical. AVFoundation decoded
that content with a `.mp4` filename and refused its extensionless cache name.
The selected first-party Swift storage repair gives ordinary MP4 a real suffix,
resolves/migrates legacy extensionless MP4, and keeps atomic writes, reads and
purges coherent across owned variants. Existing device locking, ID validation,
image storage and once exclusion remain; no app display-copy workaround or
Rust/C ABI change is introduced. Expo advances to 0.2.24 and the consumer's
Swift is rebuilt, while the Rust frameworks remain unchanged. Actual native
video/restart/revocation and final published-pin evidence are still required.


The combined 0.2.33 SDK / 0.2.24 Expo candidate then passed the actual Clique
Release application's restart, revocation and full-acquisition scenarios.
Restart includes fresh native offline image/video rendering on both process
launches, the exact two pending intents surviving death, exactly two ordered
sent settlements, and delivered/read progression. Revocation retains authorized
text history but purges ordinary media and excludes later delivery offline
and after an explicit reconnect. Full acquisition enumerates all 10,000
required message IDs in 100 scopes across 299 actual local query pages while
offline, before reconnect, matching the frozen fixture's canonical ID hash.
The [native application report](../snapback4/reports/20260922-clique-native-extension.md)
retains exact timings, package/app custody and limitations. These are candidate
results with the original 0.2.32 CLI. At that candidate checkpoint the final
published 0.2.33/0.2.24 pair still required installed-package requalification;
§9 records its subsequent completion. These observations do not change cal16's
original grade or qualify a physical device, Android, power loss or real provider.

## 9. Public-package completion and remaining provider cell

The final [actual Clique public-package qualification](../snapback4/reports/20260922-clique-native-extension.md#final-public-package-result)
passed all three unchanged native scenarios after registry installation of
SDK/CLI 0.2.33 and Expo 0.2.24, with all qualified integrities matched. The
normal Release build and its native inputs are recorded independently of the
earlier candidates. Exact offline image/video rendering, live once exclusion,
pending/settled/delivered/read pairs, learned revocation, and the 10,000-ID
offline acquisition proof all passed. The source repair, both review families,
failed attempts and final publication remain linked in the repair report.

| Increment | Actual result and boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Actual application on public native packages | Complete: three installed iOS simulator Release scenarios pass on SDK/CLI 0.2.33 and Expo 0.2.24. Physical-device, Android and power-loss evidence is absent. |
| Deployment, stopped restore and queued-job restart | Complete in the [operational record](../snapback4/reports/20260922-application-evidence.md), including isolated restore and process crash/restart. This does not establish power-loss durability. |
| Provider test doubles | Complete for recorded acceptance-before-worker-crash, stable-key deduplication and uncertain-outcome boundaries. A test-double acceptance is not a real provider or device receipt. |
| Real provider acceptance and device delivery | **Unrun**, conditional on the selected adapter, test account/device and recipient required by §4. |

This RFC stays `Accepted` for the independent real-provider increment. The
completed native and operational increments do not depend on inventing that
missing evidence. Cal16's original grade/score and all unrun labels remain
unchanged. The separate [final cold corrective-release benchmark](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-22-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.33/README.md)
is complete at 92/100, with 101 assertions passed, zero failed and 18 not run.
Its missing native artifacts remain unrun despite this RFC's separate native
qualification; its strict search midpoint rule failed (objective band 30).
