# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.


EXPOFIX's real template audit also exposed social's Router range selecting an
unqualified new patch with an unavailable dependency in npm's install view.
Pin Router 57.0.23, already in the workspace lock. The publication proof now
includes web-chat, Expo-chat and social installs/typechecks/exports as well as
the default/adapted starters. A symlinked workspace typecheck alone had missed
this installation failure.


The first clean Ubuntu run exposed another qualification-host hazard: generated
build:web used Metro's unbounded CPU-derived worker pool, which is slow under
proot. Bound generated Expo web export scripts to two workers, matching the
direct qualification command. Historical stages lacking retained Expo proof
bytes now fail explicitly and preserve all artifacts; the layout-compatibility
test is separated from the genuine-old-stage refusal.

Final review's timeout counterexample is repaired with the existing live
command-owner: async calls keep the watchdog responsive, the private pipe
requests group cleanup on success/failure/caller death, and two read-only
empty-group probes gate success without mounting host /proc in a guest.
A TERM-ignoring driver/grandchild regression verifies hard termination and
bounded return. Numeric PGIDs never authorize a runner signal. Both families
re-review this final custody change independently.
### 2026-10-13 — PAGE3 private-page end game

Party/static roles now page both on the server and full union devices;
pure memberships reuse metered fenced discovery. Positive compound ranges
retain every status predicate, including parent/status conjunctions and
nullable-enum non-null alternatives. Hidden parent sources get checked,
immutable owner/reference-copy edits with backfill and live-parent restrictions.
First independent Muse/Codex reviews found capability scope, cap exemption,
copy lifetime/reference and native/model guard-admission issues; fixes are in
26d03a6fb4 and follow-up qualification. Linux locked web measurement is
2,310,393 B raw / 704,662 B gzip, +5,258 / +1,407 versus PAGE2. Budget is
705,162 B, precisely measurement + 500 B. Core runtime ceilings stay fixed.

### 2026-10-14 — PAGE3 completed local qualification

Planner §16 and union §5.3 have Muse and fresh Codex READY in round 5 at
`65d505289f`. All earlier blockers and fixes are retained with verbatim review
provenance. Separate readable-static and read-free membership/compound model
gates now agree; static argument/signed keys retain a named model boundary.
Private lists recipes are updated and compile.

The 50 copied odd final schemas compile: 18 notes in 11 schemas becomes
2 hidden-parent notes in one. A second 50-schema corpus with the exact checked
immutable copy repair has zero notes. No original evidence lane was modified.
Final seed-0 400-case campaigns compile all sources: native 22,277 and devices
12,686 executions have no disagreements; Lean 1,108 retains only three known
C2 pagination occurrences, no new signatures. Model 74 examples/seven fixtures,
59-theorem standard-axiom audit, Lean difftests 35, smoke 18, PAGE3 language 14,
emitter 15, device branches 15 and simulator membership 9 pass. Final web/client
builds and metrology audits pass. Linux gzip 704,662 B / budget 705,162 B
retain exactly 500 B headroom. Workstream/report evidence is under
`reports/20261013-page3/`; local Charlie commits only, no push/publication.

### 2026-10-14 — Integration 10: PAGE3 after 0.4.27

Merge PAGE3 with no-ff onto ce229488de, preserving Integration 9's contextual
`not`, Lean intrinsic-id residual sampling, non-timed reader lanes, expiry-only
search authorization and grant-counter normalization. Preserve both smoke
suites; the new PAGE3 family test uses the existing optional-model helper and
keeps native coverage unconditional. The merged Lean model audits 59 theorems,
74 examples and seven fixtures. On fresh copies the 50-schema farm matches
PAGE3: two hidden-parent notes in one original schema, zero after its checked
immutable-copy repair.

Locked merged Linux wasm measures 2,313,446 B raw / 705,305 B gzip. Set
705,805 B = measurement + exactly 500 B. Whole-artifact delta versus Integration
9's final Linux artifact is +5,258 B raw / +1,127 B gzip, without per-feature
attribution or changed runtime ceilings. Retain 0.4.27: PAGE3 ships with wave-11
in 0.4.28. Mac suite is deferred to that publication; no push. Linux full
qualification and regenerated census evidence follow in a separate commit.

Integration 10 qualification repairs: the native 20k campaign found nine
opaque-cursor byte comparisons between PAGE3 direct and let-hoisted plans.
The let oracle now follows each plan's own cursor, comparing every page and
cursor presence. Its bound uses the post-prelude physical store population;
three actual regressions cover the original seed, prelude-created rows and
missing cursor/data/completeness/refusal/bad-resumption mutants. Codex round 1
found the initial seeded-row bound; round 2 and Muse approve the corrected
implementation and six-execution regression. Both verbatim rounds are retained
under llp/reviews/3000.035-integration10-cursors.*. The final native campaign
has 20k compiled cases, 1,135,558 executions and zero disagreements.

The old sequence-disclosure scan expected eight probes. PAGE3's two owner
ranges each reserve its two static probes once, totaling four independently
of hidden witness lifetimes. Retain every paired-world outcome comparison,
next-expiry assertion and the eight-probe write reservation. The repaired
existing regression passes. No engine or runtime ceiling changed.

Integration 10 also regenerates word-expansion.json via
WORD_EXPANSION_UPDATE_FIXTURE: PAGE3's pure-membership planner now merges the
blank-search browse arm, with its existing capability facts and inferred unique
source constraint. All thirteen fixture populations retain identical authorized
pages and seeded row bytes; only compiled backends change. The native generator
and no-update comparison pass. No fixture was hand-merged.

Integration 10's final full simulator run exposed a compiler interaction:
PAGE3 expanded the pure-membership empty-search browse arm, breaking the
existing paired-scan recognition and disabling nonempty search inference.
Keep compiler-generated pure-membership search variants out of branch page
expansion, using a separate site set so equality/order pin handling stays

eligible. The unchanged 99,090-row case, all four read-cut controls and ten

supersedes the earlier temporary backend changes and restores its original

list merge. Fresh Codex and Muse both mark this final patch READY.

The wave-1 missing-pin diagnostic now uses an arithmetic filter to retain its

Final rebuilt native/device/Lean campaigns retain the same totals and no new

variables. Independent review logs retain their actual commands; Codex's

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:11:## 1. Commission and scope
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:13:Charlie approved the ideal end-game release lifecycle and commissioned implementation on 2026-10-12. Implementer: this ROLL session, beginning that date. Wave 8 DT1 found additive rollback deletes new storage in all 50 day-two lanes; DT1a found unnamed enum conflicts; DT10 found deployment requires stopping serve. This supersedes the release-only removal semantics in LLP 3000.000's implementation follow-up. Development and device replacement keep their existing projection/reset semantics. No new publication authority or gate is introduced.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:15:## 2. Retain by default
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:17:Every release activation (deploy and rollback) retains removed durable application tables, columns and index definitions in SQLite-owned dormant storage within the same activation transaction. The active r/i image remains an exact projection of the active schema. Dormant data is outside that namespace and unavailable to programs, rules, references, queries, sync, SQL application projections and clients. The Quarry/IR binder admits only declared application table names; it has no general application SQL evaluator. Dormant internal names are not added to Schema, and schema validation forbids declarations in the reserved `_dormant_` and `_release_` namespaces. Inspection `snapback4 sql` is owner-only and may enumerate it. A future application SQL adapter must keep its declared authorized input allow-list. Asset reads must resolve the home table, column and row in the active schema/r image and check the current read rule; a dormant home refuses E_ASSET_UNKNOWN even when a caller knows its id. Test the reserved-name/program boundary and direct fetch by id. Reintroducing a dormant name deliberately reactivates its data, after full validation under the candidate schema. A deployment that reuses any dormant name refuses unless --restore-dormant explicitly selects restoration; its refusal lists retained items/counts/samples and the alternatives `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` or `prune <dir> --allow-destructive` before fresh creation. Rollback's selection of an immutable older release explicitly selects restoration; `rollback <dir> --to N` also selects an immutable release for deliberate roll forward. To convert durable to ephemeral, deploy a release without that table, prune it explicitly, then deploy the ephemeral definition. This applies to deployments as well as rollback; a rename remains two names, without an inferred data move.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:19:Inventory and audit entries carry a stable typed key (`table:name`, `column:table.name`, or `index:table.name`); pagination uses the returned key, not a bare path that could collide between columns and indexes. The SQLite format is `_dormant_tables(table_name, definition)` for whole-table Table definitions, `_dormant_rows(t,id,data)` for their stored images, `_dormant_columns(table_name,column_name,definition)` for retired column type/default/ownership, `_dormant_values(t,id,column_name,data)` for their cells, `_dormant_indexes(table_name,index_name,definition)` for index definitions and `_dormant_refs(t,id,column_name,parent_t,parent_id,invalid)` for deletion evidence with a parent lookup index. Catalog numbers are reserved monotonically and reused for the same physical name, but dormant rows are never kept in r under a hidden catalog number. These tables are created only during successful release activation; legacy deployments start empty and cannot recover data previously deleted by older binaries. A successful release activation writes the release-only format marker `version=snapback4-release-store-3`. New Store open/version checks accept this, the prior snapback4-release-store-2 and the legacy snapback4-store-1; old binaries reject it with E_STORE rather than opening it. Development and device stores retain snapback4-store-1 and their formats are unchanged. The format marker rolls back with failed activation. On restore, omitted cells from whole-table images move to dormant column cells before projection, merging previous retired cells; full-table entries are consumed only on commit.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:22:capabilities. Published 0.2.33 refuses retained release-store versions 2 and 3
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:25:the final data-directory explanation), including capability-free releases.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:30:new release stores. The Darwin publication fence checks both boundaries.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:37:Dormant whole tables store their row images; dormant columns store values keyed by table/column/row id and their definitions. Capture interpreted literal defaults and optional nulls when a column becomes dormant, preserving the value existing rows actually saw. Keep index definitions as history; preserve their data logically and rebuild physical keys on reactivation from current rows. Dormant indexes are never consulted for active writes, uniqueness or caps. Thus a new duplicate under the older release can precisely refuse forward activation. Derived rows are caches and are rebuilt, not authoritative history. Retention must precede existing derived-state retirement. An unsupported durable-to-ephemeral conversion refuses rather than silently discarding stored data.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:43:Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:45:## 3. Older writes and incompatible values
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:47:Updating an active row while its newer columns are dormant preserves those dormant values. Deleting that row deletes its dormant column values in the same transaction: forward never resurrects a deleted row, including when its id is reused. New rows written under the older schema have no dormant value. On forward they receive the candidate literal default or optional null; required columns without such a default refuse with exact missing-row counts and up to five ids. Repair by deploying a bridge schema with that column optional or a literal default using --restore-dormant, fill it through a bridge mutation, then deploy the desired required schema. Alternatively explicitly delete the named rows through the current release and retry. Never tell the older schema to write a field it lacks. Contextual now/viewer defaults do not invent historical values. Existing required-column backfill rules still apply.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:53:## 4. Explicit pruning and audit
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:55:snapback4 prune <dir> runs through the serving owner when live, otherwise the offline owner. It lists dormant tables, columns and indexes with row counts, logical serialized dormant bytes (including retained asset bytes separately) and at most five ids; it changes nothing without --allow-destructive. With that flag it permanently deletes exactly the currently dormant storage in one writer transaction, records a durable prune event, and leaves active rows/programs untouched. It releases dormant asset homes for the ordinary collector. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive do not prune; direct users to the separate command. Prune intentionally deletes all dormant items as one reviewed storage boundary; per-item dependency-aware pruning is deferred because partial pruning can strand dormant references and asset homes. There is no implicit size or age collector: previews make storage growth visible without inventing a data-loss policy. After prune, reintroducing the schema gets empty tables and normal required-column/default behavior. Pruning is an irreversible operator action; the command output names every item dropped.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:57:Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:61:## 5. Hot activation and ownership
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:63:serve owns the database sidecar for its lifetime and publishes a Unix-domain release-control socket in the private per-user runtime directory (§5.1; directory 0700, socket 0600). Check peer credentials on both ends (Linux SO_PEERCRED, Darwin getpeereid), requiring the effective UID. Only the host filesystem owner has this capability; it is not an HTTP door, browser origin, bearer service seat or remote endpoint. Check filesystem objects before replacing a stale socket; never follow symlinks or replace a regular file. Use `.release-command` (a hardened private regular file, no-follow, single link) for the command flock and the deterministic runtime address (§5.1) for the control socket. lstat socket entries; only a same-owner socket may be removed while holding the exclusive store sidecar. Bound command JSON to 4096 bytes and response JSON to 64 KiB, with a 5-second input timeout and 120-second command response timeout. The trusted same-UID operator can directly edit source/database; malicious same-UID edits are outside the threat model, but ordinary source races are caught by a candidate content digest over source/native/worker files in the request and verified by the server. Numeric release id is the only source selector; never a caller path. Commands hold a separate private command flock while allocating/staging immutable release source. They acquire the store owner when stopped; E_STORE_BUSY with a running serve uses the local socket instead of opening another writer. Dev and an older/unreachable server continue to refuse with an exact stop/retry command.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:65:snapback4 releases <dir> reads authoritative current release, activation revision, generation and durable events without adopting source, including while serve is live. This is the recovery command for an ambiguous result. A bounded command message identifies the owned database inode/canonical identity, candidate release id, expected current release and monotone activation revision, operation and explicit flags. The server compiles only its own immutable release directory, prepares native module/worker prerequisites before commit, then holds its existing engine writer guard for activation and in-memory publication. The server verifies both expected current release and activation revision inside the activation transaction. Increment activation revision on every successful release activation, including an equal-hash source change; A→B→A cannot satisfy an old command. Concurrent commands serialize on the command flock; out-of-band stale commands fail compare-and-swap. No request may choose arbitrary filesystem source. Compilation/validation failure leaves the previous engine, bound schema, native module, worker revision and reader factory unchanged.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:67:A query already running in a pinned SQLite read transaction finishes on its old snapshot and reports that generation. A writer already executing finishes before activation obtains its engine guard. Queued HTTP operations are admitted under one release request guard covering signature lookup, argument decoding and execution. Activation acquires its exclusive side before taking the engine guard; admitted operations finish before activation, and later operations dispatch under the new generation. Internal detached readers still fence obsolete identities with E_GENERATION. Fresh requests after publication use the new engine; a stale pooled reader not yet pinned retries through the replacement factory. Prepare a runtime bundle containing bound programs, precompiled native module/metadata, worker root and validated named declarations; prepare the reader factory from the post-activation engine while its writer guard blocks new writers (reader opening failure after commit clears the old factory and uses existing lazy retry, never aborts a committed activation or advertises the old backend). Swap bound/generation/native under the common bound guard in the existing /schema guard order, retire pooled readers, rebind ephemeral state, invalidate change answers, wake sync/polls and update worker revision while holding the writer guard. The supervisor explicitly switches its immutable release project root and named declarations on revision, including addition/removal/replacement; externally supplied --worker stays an operator-owned entry. Worker preparation captures an immutable (root, entry, declarations/environment, supervisor revision) tuple. Under the engine guard it compares that captured revision to the current worker revision before minting credentials; stale preparation is discarded and rediscovered. Never relabel an old prepared source with the latest revision. Credentials bind that checked revision so later activation fences them even when spawn finishes later. Workers restart using the candidate packaged source and native continuation hashes keep the existing conservative parking policy. Long validation can pause writes; hot means no process restart, not an unbounded zero-pause latency promise.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:69:The server continues an admitted activation even if the client hits its 120-second response timeout or closes the socket; failure to write the reply is not permission to roll back or exit the server. The database is authoritative at crash or lost socket reply. Timeout/EOF after sending a command reports an ambiguous result and instructs inspection of current and durable history; never silently resend as a new release or claim failure left the old release active. Before activation fsync required immutable source/native/worker files and their directories, then the published releases directory entry. The supported guarantee covers process crashes and filesystem/SQLite durability on hosts honoring fsync; no guarantee covers failing storage hardware. Restart attaches the database-selected release even if current's convenience copy is stale. After database commit, publication is infallible in-memory assignments and committed ephemeral rebind; fallible native compilation, declaration/environment/import validation happens before commit. Worker process spawn remains fallible after commit: failure reports committed activation with degraded worker availability and retries; it cannot undo a release or external effects. Filesystem-copy failures report the committed result.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:71:### 5.1 Path-independent control address
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:75:is 104 bytes; the previous deployment-local name failed under ordinary
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:80:SHA-256 over the raw bytes of the canonical **deployment directory**, binding
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:81:the socket to immutable release source. Aliases that canonicalize to the same path resolve
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:82:to one address; two deployments pointing to one external database retain
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:83:separate addresses, so only the serving deployment can submit its releases.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:84:At deploy and serve startup, create a missing deployment privately, resolve
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:86:release paths. Serve also resolves the selected release root before constructing
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:88:canonical active release path, including Darwin's `/var` → `/private/var` and
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:89:explicit deployment symlinks. An existing deployment alias resolves before
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:96:invocations. This deterministic address needs no durable locator file; deploy
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:97:and rollback derive exactly the same address from durable deployment location.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:98:A moved deployment must stop and restart serve, as moving its immutable source
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:121:process-global umask in a multithreaded host to hide this fail-closed window. Keep the shared runtime directory on exit:
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:122:removing it would race other deployments, and it contains no durable state.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:125:Darwin start serve, execute hot deploy and rollback without restarting, refuse
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:128:permissions/type/UID checks, canonical aliases, independent deployments, and
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:130:an incomplete control request before process exit; join the control handle
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:138:## 6. Devices and partitions
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:140:Every changed execution activation including A→B→A gets a fresh monotone semantic generation, never reuses a release number as a generation. Execution-content identity includes the prepared native module digest as well as schema/programs. A changed native digest advances the existing semantic generation atomically with release activation, even when native declarations are unchanged; removing a module changes the digest too. Equal execution content retains generation unless a release activation changes the final active/dormant population (§9.1), which requires a new replica generation; source-only activations still advance activation revision. The digest is SHA-256 over the exact prepared wasm bytes; the no-module sentinel is the empty string persisted as release_native_digest. activation_hash binds schema/programs and that digest. Comparison is with the current activation, so A→B→A is fresh. Startup attaches the persisted execution identity and verifies the immutable release module matches its recorded digest. This uses the existing device protocol, not a separate native epoch. Rollback and forward use the same generation replacement and full partition reacquisition already used by adoption: /schema advertises the exact active shape, sync rejects old watermarks, devices clear obsolete base/index state and rebase or refuse their queued intents under the existing authority/operation contract. Store id remains unchanged; this is not a store replacement. No dormant rows or fields enter snapshots, partition logs, online answers or device stores. Never combine old-generation pages with a new backend. A late old read remains tagged old and cannot overwrite a newly observed generation. Simulate rollback/forward across a delayed sync page and audience changes; retained data changes server storage only, not device persistence formats.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:142:Lock order: command flock is CLI-only; serving control never acquires it. Candidate filesystem/native/worker preparation precedes engine lock. Serving publication holds engine → bound → generation → native, and separately replaces readers while engine is held (matching existing checkout); no /schema handler takes engine while holding bound. Ephemeral rebind uses its existing committed infallible path, never a fallible post-commit store read. Update desired worker root before revision; worker thread releases root/bound guards before taking engine. Wake/seq/answers follow existing publish_engine while engine is held: these are short memory updates/nonblocking condition notifications, required before new writer admission; no socket/child wait occurs there. `releases` includes the existing worker status metadata so post-commit launch failure is visible as degraded availability. No SQLite lock is held while waiting for a child process or socket peer.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:144:## 7. Implementation and acceptance
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:146:Small commits: reviewed design; transactional retention/prune/refusals; local hot-control publication; integration/device/client coverage and docs. Authoring entry: SERVER.md owns the release-lifecycle section; guide evolution owns schema interactions. OPS guide deploy gets a one-line pointer to that section.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:148:CLI matrix: `deploy <dir> [--restore-dormant] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `rollback <dir> [--to N] [--allow-feed-adoption]`; `prune <dir> [--allow-destructive]`; `releases <dir>`. Legacy --allow-destructive on deploy/rollback is accepted but does not prune or bypass validation, and prints the prune command when relevant. --restore-dormant authorizes only restoration, never loss. --to requires a positive existing immutable release id and may select a newer release. E_ADOPT operator text: `table.column/index: N affected rows; sample ids [...]; values/keys [...]; reason; rewrite: <bridge/repair schema or command>; inspect: snapback4 sql <query> --release <dir>`. No flag bypasses invalid values, ownership or references. Unreachable live control refuses `E_STORE_BUSY: stop snapback4 serve <dir>, rerun the exact command, then snapback4 serve <dir>` before send; after send use `releases <dir>` and explicit `rollback --to N` after inspecting the outcome, never an automatic deploy allocating N+1.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:150:History/status is page-based with `releases <dir> [--after <event-id>]`; bounded pages return a continuation and inventory pages use stable item keys. Prune audit stores a complete item list in SQLite; bounded replies carry a continuation rather than truncate silently. Critical assertions include T(a,b) → absent T → T(a) → absent T → T(a,b), preserved b and latest updated a; identical parent id/value replacement invalidates dormant edges; stale revision after A→B→A and equal-hash activation refuses; activation-time parent expiry followed by identical-id replacement invalidates dormant references; rapid B→C activation with a barrier between B worker preparation and credential minting discards B conventional/named source; an old checked-out reader not yet pinned retries the new factory including native-only changes; a connected client holding native module A hot-adopts B with unchanged declarations, reacquires B backend/module and refuses a delayed A result; inject interruption before/after commit and during pointer-copy updates to distinguish no activation from committed activation with an ambiguous result.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:152:Tests also cover private socket permissions/symlinks/peer credentials, racing commands and writes during validation, process crash before commit and between commit/publication/reply, live prune/status, legacy empty dormancy and unchanged device format. Tests: deploy v1/write, v2 adding table/required default/enum/index/write, precise refusal while new enum is in use, authorized repair then rollback with dormant table/column, old update/insert/delete, forward restores prior values and supplies defaults only to new rows. Also prune preview/confirmation and asset lifetime; unique failure after dormant-index writes; required default-less forward refusal; transaction failure leaves active and dormant data intact; restart binds the committed release; simultaneous hot requests and failed hot activation; stale-generation sync and real client reacquisition; simulator delayed pages/audiences. Quality bar: the commissioned core/lang/device/sim cargo suites, CLI release/serve/adoption tests, relevant client tests and ref-check. No data-loss claim rests on diagnostics alone.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:154:## 8. Review disposition and continuation
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:160:## 9. Implementation record
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:162:The core implements dormant tables/cells/index definitions and reference-lifetime evidence, partial restoration, required-value/enum/unique/cap refusal populations, prune/history, release-only format fencing, revision CAS and native digest in activation_hash. Nineteen focused retention regressions pass, including activation-time expiry, assets, typed audit pagination and ownership refusal.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:164:The host implements the private same-owner transport, prepared native/worker custody, atomic live engine publication, reader fencing, revision-bound worker preparation, named-worker mutation and database-authoritative crash recovery. Seven focused hot-activation tests and four transport tests pass; the complete host unit suite passes (393 tests plus four existing ignored). All 23 release CLI/client integration tests pass, including an already-connected native-only client, concurrent traffic, hot narrowing refusal and process death before/after commit. Restoring full tables installs the identity scan before read-through adoption. Historical recorded attribution (including nulls), parent bindings and captured deadlines survive restoration; only physically missing captures are filled, and effective deadlines recompute under the candidate. An older release may make a through reference mutable; restoration refuses if its current parent table/id differs from the retained binding. Both naming orders of joint parent/child retirement, an unswept expired attribution target, effective deadline visibility, partial capture deletion/reuse and older retargeting are regressions. A test-only publication barrier permits an owned subprocess kill after commit and before runtime/pointer/reply publication, followed by database-authoritative recovery and retained forward restoration. Named secret selections are validated in immutable preparation before commit. The device and simulator exercise delayed old snapshots and audiences on both stores. SERVER.md owns the lifecycle semantics, the evolution card supplies the schema bridge, and the OPS deploy card is a one-line pointer. Final full quality results and commits are recorded in WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No publication is claimed.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:167:Final complete core/lang/device/simulator command finished with exit 0. The 159-case consolidated client scope finished with 158 passing, one existing optional 0.2.32 interoperability skip and zero failures against an immutable built CLI. Final CLI, web/package and ref-check results are recorded in WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. Internal name reservation is limited to `_dormant_` and `_release_`: established application underscore naming remains supported, while catalog-number resolution into r/i isolates physical internal tables. Both independent amendment reviewers returned READY. The implemented RFC is archived by removing its current/ link; this file remains at its permanent path.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:169:## 8. Integration with the loop (2026-10-12)
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:175:action. If the reference remains active, the selected release's action applies,

llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:190:OPS's deploy card retains account/admin/migration/port recipes and points to
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:191:SERVER.md#release-lifecycle for hot activation, retention, explicit restore/prune
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:195:the serving deployment's immutable-source directory. Another deployment sharing
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:202:### Control socket portability qualification (2026-10-12)
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:214:on each host. Counts exclude nested subprocess summaries. Each host also
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:215:passes all nine named targets (267 tests), the focused release filter (42
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:229:## 9. Wave 9 release lifecycle amendment
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:231:Implementer: REL, 2026-10-12. This supersedes §3's narrowing refusal and §5's exposed request-generation race for release serving. HAND owns device version negotiation; DX owns production-writer diagnostics and starter/password UI.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:233:### 9.1 Per-row narrowed-value dormancy
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:235:Each actual release activation, including roll-forward and equal-schema/program-only/native-only selection, runs the row-visibility closure. A visibility change advances generation even if execution hashes are equal, so devices replace their population; identical-source deploy no-ops do not activate. The closure removes authoritative rows whose **present** values do not fit the candidate column type or text constraint (asset leaves include ledger-backed kind, byte bound and file MIME allow-list, recursively through optionals/lists) into `_dormant_narrow_rows(t,id,data,definition,reason)`. Store format advances to `snapback4-release-store-3`; this binary opens format 2 and upgrades only during committed activation. Absent new required fields remain E_ADOPT. Engine metadata and maintained cache fields are not a narrowing trigger. Store the full authoritative image and its source table definition (merge restored whole-table and column definitions before classifying restored values), with interpreted old literal defaults and nulls, before projection. Restore any retained image that fits the candidate, splitting omitted columns into existing dormant cells. Restoration is automatic even on deploy; this is an existing row lifetime, not reuse of a removed schema name. No fallback syntax ships: the declared policy is hidden rows, never value substitution.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:237:Visibility is a fixed point: a row with a non-null reference (including nested/list references) to a dormant narrowed row is also dormant. This covers authorization parents and memberships. Active programs, rules, point reads, lists, counts, caps, maintained views, assets and application SQL see only active r/i. Tentative restore-and-rehide work does not count as a visibility change; compute population change from newly hidden rows and successfully restored rows in the final closed population. All dependent physical indexes and aggregates rebuild when rows enter or leave dormancy, including otherwise unchanged tables. Sync generation replacement clears base/index images and acquisition, then reacquires only that same active population. This supersedes the device literal-default image-retention optimization: an additive default can restore a dormant cell whose actual value is incompatible, so schema shape alone cannot certify an old image. Same-generation attachment and identical-source no-ops preserve images; outbox custody survives generation replacement. HAND owns the shipped-device compatibility floor; DX owns updates to client/evolution guide text. A reference lookup to a dormant id is absent. Preserve LLP 3000.039 §1/§4.4: references validate a nominal id, not target existence. A new write may carry a dormant id just as it may carry an unknown/hidden id; read-through or explicit require/insert rules decide target admission. Such a write remains active under the old release until the next adoption's reference-closure pass, but grants no parent-derived authority. Never probe dormancy selectively at the reference write boundary, which would be an existence oracle. Dormant rows contribute neither to uniqueness nor caps. Restoration uses the same set-null cap exemption as ordinary writes; null-exempt groups are never counted by adoption or successor assessment. Their ids remain reserved: inserts cannot replace them and return the same E_RULE as a hidden live identity conflict, without row contents or a special dormant code. Updates/deletes by old programs see an absent row; active updates retain unrelated dormant cells. Dormant uniqueness keys are not reserved: old writes may cause a constraint conflict on forward, which E_ADOPT reports with executable application SQL; resolve that conflict under the current release when those rows are active. If both conflict rows are dormant, deploy a bridge with compatible widened types and the conflicting index non-unique/cap increased using --restore-dormant, restore both, repair with authorized mutations, then reinstate the constraint. Neither row silently wins; activation refuses atomically. Diagnostics include this bridge and application SQL, with values only in privileged operator output (§3). Type incompatibility alone never blocks switching.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:239:At one pinned activation time, retire affected expired predecessor read-through rows under their old deadlines before narrowed hiding; retirement uses the ordinary authoritative delete to invalidate dependent evidence and remove dormant cells. Then restore candidate-compatible images, hide the incompatible/reference-dependent population, rebuild all physical r/i and _cap_counts/maintained caches, and validate before publication. Counts, sums and private parent-column mirrors all use this order. Reference closure removes at least one active row per iteration, terminates on the finite store even with nominal cycles, and any non-null element of a nested/list reference can hide its row. Null has no edge; a missing ordinary nominal target alone does not trigger hiding, while an absent target schema or narrowed-dormant target does. Capture dormant reference lifetime evidence before hiding; hiding is not deletion. Actual parent deletion invalidates evidence. Transferring a retained whole row or cell into narrowed dormancy preserves its existing bound/unbound/deleted evidence; it must never recapture a replacement parent. On candidate restoration, evidence invalidated by an actual deletion keeps a row dormant only if its reference/capture column is candidate-visible. An initially dangling nominal keep reference captures invalid=2 (unbound), distinct from invalid=1 (deleted captured lifetime); it may restore without inventing a lifetime. Deletion invalidates only bound (invalid=0) edges. Omitted reference cells and evidence stay retained for future restoration. A narrowed row never rebinds a deleted visible parent. Restored read-through images retain their historical captures and must pass the existing audience/deadline safety check. Whole-table restoration keeps the full source image through lifetime retirement and narrowed classification; ordinary candidate projection follows that boundary. Before projecting a narrowed image, evaluate retirement against its retained source definition/deadline, including direct expiry without read-through; removing read-through or expiry cannot revive an expired source lifetime. Actual retirement deletes the narrowed image, dependent captured evidence and its retained asset homes. Rows whose target table is absent stay dormant. Dormant rows run no jobs/expiry until reactivation. Assets remain retained but inaccessible. `prune --allow-destructive` drops narrowed images, associated dormant cells and lifetime evidence and releases only their asset homes; the preview names counts/bytes/sample ids. Inventory uses `rows:<table>` typed keys and audit items record before_rows, after_rows, restored_rows and bounded before_sample_ids in the activation transaction, including partial column/row restorations whose typed key remains. Per-table narrowed restoration counters distinguish restore-and-hide swaps with equal totals; summary restored_items includes partial restorations. Restoration identity sets are held transactionally and counted against final surviving r only after all read-through/cache activation phases. Column restored counts are recorded from cells actually consumed into surviving candidate rows, not inferred from inventory decreases; expiry/deletion may remove cells without restoring them. There is no automatic collection.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:241:### 9.2 Exact preview and operator vocabulary
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:243:`rollback <dir> --to N --check` and `check --store <path> --release N` execute candidate adoption on a private SQLite snapshot created by VACUUM INTO through a read-only source connection. This is disk-backed, uses the exact adoption builder/validation, and never changes the live source, pointer, events or generation. SQLite supports VACUUM INTO on a read-only source; it acquires a coherent WAL read snapshot without the source writer lock. BUSY/disk failures refuse with retry advice, never fall back to copying sidecars. The temporary directory is owner-only and removed on success, refusal and ordinary unwinding. Process kill/crash may leave a private preview directory; operators can remove snapback4-release-preview-* leftovers when no preview uses them. The live format, including format 2, is never upgraded by preview. The snapshot may require disk/time proportional to the store; it provides a point-in-time plan, not a write freeze. The output enumerates the full candidate inventory and totals every narrowed population, not just the first sixteen status items; it includes any E_ADOPT refusal. Preview prepares the candidate native content identity but never starts workers or effects.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:245:Deploy warns when type widening (including image/video byte bounds and file MIME/byte sets) admits values the previous release refuses; rollback also warns when its selected target admits values the current release refuses. All E_ADOPT inspect SQL uses quoted application table identifiers accepted by `sql`, including Quarry names that are SQL keywords; internal dormant inventory is inspected through `releases`. `latest` denotes the highest complete immutable source release, independently of current release and monotone generation. Complete here means a numeric directory with nonempty source that compiles; empty permanently reserved crash directories are skipped. Native preparation still validates a selected release before activation. Packaging and comparing the content digest precedes allocating a numeric release. Identical packaged source is a no-op: no new release directory, activation revision, audit or generation. A no-op is not an activation and therefore does not advance §5's activation revision. Widening warnings go to stderr, are advisory, and do not change a successful exit status. `releases <dir>` adds a JSON latest field; `releases <dir> [N|latest]` adds selected source id/digest/currentness without changing the authoritative current identity or activating; `rollback --to latest` and preview `--release latest` accept it. Command completion follows in-memory publication and prints `release N is current as generation G`.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:247:### 9.3 HTTP admission and reporting
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:251:Buffer bounded request bytes before final release admission. A fair release request lease then spans each one-shot /q and /m's release-dependent lookup, typed argument decoding, execution and reply-identity capture. Release it before serialization/socket output, including every refusal/replay path. A scoped cleanup releases it on unwinding/early exits. Queued exclusive activation prevents new shared admissions, avoiding writer starvation. Release activation takes the exclusive lease across adoption and publication. Acquire it before engine/bound/readers locks. Sync and held polls retain their existing generation negotiation and wake behavior, so a long poll cannot block release publication. The guard admits concurrent readers; activation may pause new operations while it waits for current ones. Dev retains predecessor snapshot reading during adoption. Each engine JSON reply carries `generation`; custom application route bodies remain byte-owned by the application and carry the header. query, schema and sync envelopes that already specify their pinned generation retain that value; replay and generic refusals use the admitted request generation. Authentication, asset preparation/admission (including early refusals), durable actions/effects, service minting and ephemeral work share an engine-work accessor that stamps under the actual producer lock after buffered intake. Authentication enablement is rechecked against that same engine schema at credential collection/mint, including after KDF; successful ancillary credential checks and commit notifications do not relabel a captured result. Bound-only/raw metadata captures its own bundle identity. Durable actions stamp the engine that admits or collects after buffered intake, without holding a release lease during worker waits. Raw native/asset/following/application-route responses use the header, without modifying owned bytes. Stamp it while holding the engine that authorizes/collects the result, after body intake; a request-arrival stamp alone cannot label a later engine generation. The HTTP X-Snapback-Generation header carries the same value. Stream/asset replies use the header. Unknown operations return 404 denied E_OP; typed mutation argument decoding and range failures return 200 failed E_INPUT (with the request id when supplied); query argument failures and malformed JSON retain their 400 denied input envelope. No one-shot release operation exposes adoption loading images or E_GENERATION.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:253:Default adoption output is one measured receipt line; phase/cache details are exposed with --verbose/--json. The check adoption reporter emits N_ADOPTION_PAUSE only when estimated work exceeds one second (explicit verbose/json retains the small-store estimate). Large unestimated work is still described in verbose output; no estimate is an SLO. guide deploy owns rollback-after-widening, business compatibility and persona-to-password onboarding text, with pointers to DX-owned first-writer checks/starters.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:256:### 9.4 Round-one review dispositions
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:258:Muse (muse-spark-1.3-contributor, high) and a fresh GPT-6.1-sol (high) found the initial amendment NOT READY. Their verbatim records are under llp/reviews. Adopted: finite reference closure and rebuild ordering; ordinary hidden-id refusal; concrete bridge repair when both unique conflicts are dormant; VACUUM read-snapshot/error/crash semantics; precise latest/no-op/warning vocabulary; input buffering and reply lease release before socket I/O. GPT's nominal-reference objection is resolved by preserving LLP 3000.039's no-existence-check law. Its lifetime objections are resolved by separating initially dangling evidence, ignoring candidate-omitted edges, and retiring predecessor expiry before hiding. Round-two reviewers receive the revised amendment and implementation independently.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:261:Round two: Muse READY; GPT NOT READY on four implementation gaps. Fixed and tested: prune/inventory asset homes now match retained row identities, equal-schema activations rerun closure and force a generation plus cache rebuild on population change, typed mutation decoding uses the promised failed envelope, and previews enumerate all inventory items. The GPT example alleging enum string rejection in wire::from_json was not accurate (the function converts text, and the engine validates the enum), but the broader wrong-shape/extra-argument envelope finding was valid and was fixed; the stress test now asserts the old-release enum failure unconditionally. Added focused tests for partial restoration/prune, legacy format-2 deletion evidence, equal-schema nominal references, mutation decoding and large inventories. Round three reviews the clarified amendment and these deltas independently.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:278:Round ten: Muse READY; GPT found asset byte/kind/MIME incompatibilities bypassing dormancy and later refusing activation. Classification now shares the existing ledger-backed asset validator for scalar/optional/list leaves on hiding and restoration. Author also verified a legal table named values broke unquoted inspection SQL; every hint now quotes the table identifier, with a live SQL regression. MEDIA retains upload/outbox ownership; this is release adoption only.
llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md:280:Round eleven: Muse READY; GPT found auth/guest minting under a new engine with the intake generation label. Producer capture is now shared across authentication and remaining worker/metadata paths, with intake-switch regressions. Explicit releases N/latest inspection fulfills the literal selector request in addition to the latest listing field.

llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2756:## 8. Implementation map
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2765:| `loopbar/` | the loop-bar brief (reduced Clique M1–M3, four-part proof) and the serial runner with independent verification → `metrics/loopbar.jsonl`; **the bar met on 0.0.11** (3/3 green in 443–537 s, no attributed loop; record under `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-12-snapback4-loopbar/`) | `11c5202532`, the loop-bar landing |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2766:| `crates/snapback4` | `init`, `check [--cost]`, `dev` (serve + watch + adopt with the day-two class), `run`, `test [--fixture]` (meters beside ceilings), `why`, `guide`, `data`, `doctor`, `deploy`, `rollback`, `serve` (a release, tokens only); the HTTP wire (`/q`, `/m`, `/sync`, `/changes`, `/schema`, `/seed`, `/auth/*`) | `b7449cdaac`, then H0 |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2769:| `packages/snapback4/src/local.ts`, `web.ts`, `native.ts`, `backend.ts`, `auth.ts` | the one client over one device interface (`DeviceOpener`: `open(name, backend)` → one JSON call): the session, the link, the transport, the observation of reads; three openers — `webDevice()` (the wasm device, memory with IndexedDB written behind each call, one device per tab, an orphaned tab's queued writes adopted), `nativeDevice` (`snapback4-expo`), `processDevice()` (Node); the TypeScript interpreter, replica and stores retired (the ruling of 2026-09-13); the lifecycle suite runs on both the wasm and the process tier | the one-implementation landing |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2782:| `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-13-snapback4-clique-0003/` | the Clique hub run on 0.1.0, the whole brief, nothing struck: every milestone green in 7,002 s (M8 at 6,926 s), graded 84/1/16 of 101 (O1 62, O2 48, O4 35, O5 band 30), M7 capability-failure on the app's pre-existing logout crash after delivering its polls and migration; eight fix-forward releases the same night (0.1.1–0.1.8: one thread per request, asset facts on the argument, `a if cond else b`, `sum(page, .x)`, a one-second clock, the paged snapshot with `N_NO_HORIZON`, the outbox in submission order, `N_WIDE_MERGE`); then what the run said to do next: the `feeds` card (fan-out on write, compiled and run in `tests/clique.rs`), `onSession`/`useSession` for the sign-out contract the app's logout crash exposed, and the simulator witness proving a cold start offline and writes composed offline surviving a restart on SQLite | the run's landings, 0.1.12 |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2786:| `packages/snapback4-expo`, `apps/chat-expo/src/client.ts`, `witness/expo.mjs` (`WITNESS_DEV_CLIENT=1`) | the Expo module: Swift over the C ABI, the Rust library as a 9.1 MB xcframework (staticlib alone, stripped; 3.3 MB on npm), `nativeAvailable()` choosing the tier at import; the simulator witness on a development build passes the five proofs on the module (303 rows synced in 31 ms, reopen 6 ms, cold start offline 2 ms) → `metrics/witness.jsonl` `dev-client-native`; `snapback4-expo@0.1.14` on npm | the Expo module landing |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2788:| `crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/native.rs`, `crates/snapback4-native`, `crates/snapback4/src/native.rs`, `native-fixtures/rescore`, `crates/snapback4-sim/tests/native.rs` | native jobs (LLP 3000.005): the wasm host over the chunk's `Runner` (get, lookup, scan, mint, insert, insert-if-absent, update, upsert, delete, log, now), one chunk per call with the module's state in the job row, fuel per chunk, refusals and traps parking the job; the guest SDK (`Ctx`, `Chunk`, `Next`, `jobs!`, `from_row`/`row_of`); `check` writing `native.rs` and building `native.wasm`, `dev` rebuilding on save, `deploy` shipping the module, `doctor` and `check --cost` reporting it; the fixture: Interview's rescore in Rust with a float exponent, resumed across three chunks, plus a write past the ceiling and a panic, each parked with its reason | the native-jobs landing |

snapback4/reports/20260930-bn-envelope-proof/round1/round0-controls.log:434:    actual: '//! The bounds card. Quarry examples are compiled by tests/guide.rs.\n// HV web figures: reports/20260929-hv-heavy-acquisition/REPORT.md, five-run table.\npub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Bounds and the meter\n\nThe meter limits the work each read, mutation and job transaction can do.\nDeclare product limits; the engine chooses execution bounds within those ceilings.\n\n```quarry\ntable scalePosts:\n  body: text <=500\n  public\n  online only\nquery scalePage(c: cursor ?):\n  return scalePosts first 20 by .id after c\nquery scalePopulation():\n  return count(scalePosts)\n```\n\nWrite product limits: a page size, a text length, an asset size or a real\nper-group cap. Do not choose an index, a merge width or a job transaction\nsize. Predicate reads and views choose their plans; top-level job `for`\nloops choose their chunks. Removing a written number never removes the meter.\n\nA query\'s `for row in t where .owner = viewer:` and a merge lane\'s source may omit `first N`\nwhen the read rule is uniformly decided by that prefix; they read ascending\nuntil the prefix ends or the meter cuts, and `check --cost` prints `rows ≤ meter`\nand `lanes ≤ meter` when no fitting schema cap supplies a static bound.\nA loop the meter cuts has no continuation; a total over a group that can outgrow one read is a maintained `view`.\nA returned or bound page instead needs a schema cap or an explicit page bound.\nJobs may iterate uncapped domains under the chunk\'s meter. Top-level job\n`for` sweeps shrink their transaction size after a meter refusal. Mutation loops\nstill need a schema-capped group that fits the examined ceiling. Direct\n`count(domain)`, `sum(domain, .column)` and `exists domain` stream under the\nmeter without retaining the page. Schema-capped query `count` and `sum` with\nnon-uniform read rules keep their bounded page execution and coverage contract.\nMutations retain their uniform-read and\nplain-prefix admission rules; a cut aborts the transaction, never commits a\npartial total. A streaming query total with a meter cut or unknown device\ncoverage leaves the client read `loading`, never a partial count or sum.\nUnknown earlier reads also make a streaming scalar unavailable, including\ninputs saved in locals or used as domain bounds; a fallback cannot establish\ncoverage.\n\n## Allocation and completeness\n\nHeap is a cumulative allocation and binding budget. Streaming aggregates\nrelease each transient row and its key scratch; one cursor allowance remains.\nInner job domains decode one prepaid row at a time and retain each selected\nrow under the heap meter before fetching another. Once decoded, the row\'s\nretained allowance replaces its decode scratch. Retained rows, their vector\nand the live cursor stay charged; domains do not retain unused result keys.\nConstructed values,\nconcatenation and other allocating builtins, local bindings, and owned\ncopies count. Inspecting a held value\'s length or sum borrows it; row and\nmap clones share their immutable image. A heap cut stops a query immediately\nwith no available value or continuation, since an interrupted scalar or shape\nhas no valid partial answer. The client read is `loading`; narrow the query or\nuse a maintained view for a total. Its cut site remains internal;\nthe meter is available only through dev\'s default read inspection (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §4).\nAn unfinished maintained lookup or live aggregate uses the same unavailable\nquery result, preserving zero only for a completed missing-group lookup.\nA mutation rolls back; Operations below describes the refusal.\nA page whose read rule the prefix does not decide (the deciding fields include a\ncolumn the scan does not pin) is never `complete`: the server cannot tell\n"no more" from "more you may not see". Pin the deciding columns in the predicate, or read the page on the device (`guide sync`), where the\npartition holds exactly what the viewer may read. `check` says so\n(`N_NEVER_COMPLETE`), as it notes a query page returned without a cursor\n(`N_NO_CURSOR`), a synced table whose groups can grow with no horizon\n(`N_NO_HORIZON`), and a merge past 50 lanes (`N_WIDE_MERGE`).\n`complete` means the query answered its declared request within known coverage and execution limits.\nFilling `first N` or `last N` is complete, including a composed preview.\nA cursorless page cannot continue; declare `c: cursor ?` and use `after c`\nif the screen pages. Composed fields and pages covering a fitting group cap\nomit `N_NO_CURSOR`.\n\n## Operations\n\nNothing is priced at check. As a program runs, the meter counts examined\nrows, index entries, rule probes, steps, heap bytes, output bytes and rows\nwritten against ceilings: 8,192 rows examined, 16,384 index entries, 8,192\nrule probes, 1,000,000 steps, 16 MiB heap, 1 MiB output, 1,024 rows written\nper mutation; a job\'s chunk may examine 16,384 rows and write 8,192. A\nquery past a ceiling returns what it found with `complete: false`; a\nmutation aborts with E_BOUND naming the meter, the ceiling and the site\n(never the count: it would measure rows the viewer may not read).\nRead a smaller page, narrow its predicate, or move the sweep into a job.\nAuthorization-dependent scans prepay a fixed logical allowance before row\ndiscovery. Hidden candidates do not consume the caller\'s remaining allowance;\nreadable rows, filters and output still do. These logical meters exclude private\nscan work: authorization candidates share one physical limit of 8,192\nper request, including nested witnesses and maintained-source walks. A row behind at least 8,192 such candidates is unreachable by paging\nin that direction; an empty page has no cursor into the hidden range. Expiring\ncap checks draw from that same allowance; any authorization that cannot be\nestablished within it returns generic E_RULE without identifying a cause.\nThe device\'s separate prediction-work budget can instead queue an operation.\n`snapback4 check --cost` prints each site\'s structural class;\n`snapback4 test` prints the numbers on your data and names query/persona reads\npast half any ceiling without a cut: at most ten lines and a count of the rest.\nLoopback `dev` logs actual meter and word cuts with the operation, principal,\nsite, counters and `snapback4 why <op>`. Repeats are suppressed per operation\nand site (at most two lines per minute); the next emitted line includes the\nsuppressed count. `serve` neither logs these costs nor returns read meters.\nA loop is bounded\nby a page\'s `first N`, a schema-capped prefix, a query\'s meter-bounded prefix, a list\'s `<=N`, `range`\'s int expressions, `walk`\'s depth,\nor `tokens`\' 64. An accumulated list is bounded by the heap meter and its\niterations by the step meter. `while` stays refused.\n`range(a, b)` evaluates int expressions once and iterates under the step meter,\nwithout materializing the range. `check --cost` prints `steps ≤ meter`, a\nfiltered scan as `filter over ≤ examined ceiling`, its reads as `probe × examined`,\nand a seek as a prefix. Sorting or grouping a page only processes that page.\n`sha256(text)` charges the input\'s UTF-8 byte length plus one to steps before\nhashing, then 64 heap bytes for its fixed lowercase hexadecimal result. A step\ncut refuses a scalar query with E_BOUND; a cut on that result allocation leaves\nthe client read `loading`, as other query heap cuts do. A mutation rolls\nback on either cut.\n\n### Prove the heaviest account\n\nProve the envelope\'s heaviest account, not a typical one: 500 follows and\n1,000 conversations need that account\'s acquisition and cold restart evidence.\nAgainst your running backend, replace alice and heldNotes with your heaviest\npersona and a device query; SB4_URL is dev\'s printed URL, SB4_PROOF a new directory:\n\n```sh\nsnapback4 witness acquire --as alice --url "$SB4_URL" --query heldNotes --restart --artifacts "$SB4_PROOF" --json\n```\n\nFor serve or a real account, use `--as <principal> --token <bearer>`.\nOptional `--args \'{"c":null}\'` supplies query arguments; without `--query` the\nwitness selects the first zero-argument device query. `--runs 3` uses three fresh\nstores. Default URL is http://127.0.0.1:4400; `--timeout 120` bounds each run.\nThe command writes acquire.json even on failure; --json prints it, otherwise\none summary per run. Receipt excerpt (counts depend on your fixture):\n\n```json\n{"passed":true,"runs":[{"coverage":{"groups":1,"rows":2},"restart":{"retained":true,"downloadedRows":0}}]}\n```\n\nIt uses the native process device, the same SQLite module as Expo. Web wasm +\nIndexedDB figures differ. `acquiredMs` ends at the SDK acquired boundary;\n`firstLocalMs` times the first complete local query after that boundary, both\nfrom fresh startup including identity checks. Restart firstLocalMs includes cold\nreopen with all HTTP blocked. Retained means identical store/generation/watermark,\nper-group row counts and persisted coverage, and table row/byte counts before\nreconnect; reconnect must then send no row events (an empty snapshot is possible\nat watermark zero). Keep the fixture\nstable: new writes or expiry can invalidate this comparison. No OS-cache flush,\napp UI, media-cache or physical-phone performance is claimed.\nGroups are table/group pairs with rows or recorded coverage (plus implicit public\nand principal groups); inspect groupsByTable.messages for the conversation count.\nresidency.totalBytes is SQLite pages, excluding WAL/SHM, not RSS; tables give row\npayload bytes. requests preserve Server-Timing and parsed serverMs; serverSyncMs\nsums acquisition sync total durations (null on serve). Verification scans follow\nthe timed answer. Background polling is suppressed; SDK sync itself is unchanged.\nHV\'s measured web guidance on eb1000 (five-run medians, 2026-09-29): 1,000\nmemberships / 105,034 rows acquired in 18.609 s default or 19.522 s Worker;\nfirst inbox 0.441 / 0.520 s, about 307.6 MB allocated wasm memory. These are\nhistorical workload measurements, not a promise or native-device numbers.\n\n### Measuring a growth curve\n\nUse the same backend, query arguments, principal, build and host at each corpus\ncheck'... 16504 more characters,

snapback4/reports/20260929-np-macos-slice/round7/REPORT.md:22:| Missing snapshot custody regression | The Xcode fixture changes only snapshot JavaScript from B to C before bundling; live source stays B and the produced HBC contains C. Preparation must fail with `E_WITNESS_PROOF: snapshot source changed during preparation` and no `build.json`. Removing only that existing guard makes preparation exit zero and the regression fail. |
snapback4/reports/20260929-np-macos-slice/round7/tools/mutations.py:28:        guard = "  if (inventory(snapshot, sourceOmit).sha256 !== sourceSnapshot.sha256) fail('E_WITNESS_PROOF', 'snapshot source changed during preparation');\n"
