/tmp/sb4-w11-proof-reviews/codex-r1.txt:2288:snapback4/reports/20260930-bn-envelope-proof/round1/round0-controls.log:434:    actual: '//! The bounds card. Quarry examples are compiled by tests/guide.rs.\n// HV web figures: reports/20260929-hv-heavy-acquisition/REPORT.md, five-run table.\npub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Bounds and the meter\n\nThe meter limits the work each read, mutation and job transaction can do.\nDeclare product limits; the engine chooses execution bounds within those ceilings.\n\n```quarry\ntable scalePosts:\n  body: text <=500\n  public\n  online only\nquery scalePage(c: cursor ?):\n  return scalePosts first 20 by .id after c\nquery scalePopulation():\n  return count(scalePosts)\n```\n\nWrite product limits: a page size, a text length, an asset size or a real\nper-group cap. Do not choose an index, a merge width or a job transaction\nsize. Predicate reads and views choose their plans; top-level job `for`\nloops choose their chunks. Removing a written number never removes the meter.\n\nA query\'s `for row in t where .owner = viewer:` and a merge lane\'s source may omit `first N`\nwhen the read rule is uniformly decided by that prefix; they read ascending\nuntil the prefix ends or the meter cuts, and `check --cost` prints `rows ≤ meter`\nand `lanes ≤ meter` when no fitting schema cap supplies a static bound.\nA loop the meter cuts has no continuation; a total over a group that can outgrow one read is a maintained `view`.\nA returned or bound page instead needs a schema cap or an explicit page bound.\nJobs may iterate uncapped domains under the chunk\'s meter. Top-level job\n`for` sweeps shrink their transaction size after a meter refusal. Mutation loops\nstill need a schema-capped group that fits the examined ceiling. Direct\n`count(domain)`, `sum(domain, .column)` and `exists domain` stream under the\nmeter without retaining the page. Schema-capped query `count` and `sum` with\nnon-uniform read rules keep their bounded page execution and coverage contract.\nMutations retain their uniform-read and\nplain-prefix admission rules; a cut aborts the transaction, never commits a\npartial total. A streaming query total with a meter cut or unknown device\ncoverage leaves the client read `loading`, never a partial count or sum.\nUnknown earlier reads also make a streaming scalar unavailable, including\ninputs saved in locals or used as domain bounds; a fallback cannot establish\ncoverage.\n\n## Allocation and completeness\n\nHeap is a cumulative allocation and binding budget. Streaming aggregates\nrelease each transient row and its key scratch; one cursor allowance remains.\nInner job domains decode one prepaid row at a time and retain each selected\nrow under the heap meter before fetching another. Once decoded, the row\'s\nretained allowance replaces its decode scratch. Retained rows, their vector\nand the live cursor stay charged; domains do not retain unused result keys.\nConstructed values,\nconcatenation and other allocating builtins, local bindings, and owned\ncopies count. Inspecting a held value\'s length or sum borrows it; row and\nmap clones share their immutable image. A heap cut stops a query immediately\nwith no available value or continuation, since an interrupted scalar or shape\nhas no valid partial answer. The client read is `loading`; narrow the query or\nuse a maintained view for a total. Its cut site remains internal;\nthe meter is available only through dev\'s default read inspection (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §4).\nAn unfinished maintained lookup or live aggregate uses the same unavailable\nquery result, preserving zero only for a completed missing-group lookup.\nA mutation rolls back; Operations below describes the refusal.\nA page whose read rule the prefix does not decide (the deciding fields include a\ncolumn the scan does not pin) is never `complete`: the server cannot tell\n"no more" from "more you may not see". Pin the deciding columns in the predicate, or read the page on the device (`guide sync`), where the\npartition holds exactly what the viewer may read. `check` says so\n(`N_NEVER_COMPLETE`), as it notes a query page returned without a cursor\n(`N_NO_CURSOR`), a synced table whose groups can grow with no horizon\n(`N_NO_HORIZON`), and a merge past 50 lanes (`N_WIDE_MERGE`).\n`complete` means the query answered its declared request within known coverage and execution limits.\nFilling `first N` or `last N` is complete, including a composed preview.\nA cursorless page cannot continue; declare `c: cursor ?` and use `after c`\nif the screen pages. Composed fields and pages covering a fitting group cap\nomit `N_NO_CURSOR`.\n\n## Operations\n\nNothing is priced at check. As a program runs, the meter counts examined\nrows, index entries, rule probes, steps, heap bytes, output bytes and rows\nwritten against ceilings: 8,192 rows examined, 16,384 index entries, 8,192\nrule probes, 1,000,000 steps, 16 MiB heap, 1 MiB output, 1,024 rows written\nper mutation; a job\'s chunk may examine 16,384 rows and write 8,192. A\nquery past a ceiling returns what it found with `complete: false`; a\nmutation aborts with E_BOUND naming the meter, the ceiling and the site\n(never the count: it would measure rows the viewer may not read).\nRead a smaller page, narrow its predicate, or move the sweep into a job.\nAuthorization-dependent scans prepay a fixed logical allowance before row\ndiscovery. Hidden candidates do not consume the caller\'s remaining allowance;\nreadable rows, filters and output still do. These logical meters exclude private\nscan work: authorization candidates share one physical limit of 8,192\nper request, including nested witnesses and maintained-source walks. A row behind at least 8,192 such candidates is unreachable by paging\nin that direction; an empty page has no cursor into the hidden range. Expiring\ncap checks draw from that same allowance; any authorization that cannot be\nestablished within it returns generic E_RULE without identifying a cause.\nThe device\'s separate prediction-work budget can instead queue an operation.\n`snapback4 check --cost` prints each site\'s structural class;\n`snapback4 test` prints the numbers on your data and names query/persona reads\npast half any ceiling without a cut: at most ten lines and a count of the rest.\nLoopback `dev` logs actual meter and word cuts with the operation, principal,\nsite, counters and `snapback4 why <op>`. Repeats are suppressed per operation\nand site (at most two lines per minute); the next emitted line includes the\nsuppressed count. `serve` neither logs these costs nor returns read meters.\nA loop is bounded\nby a page\'s `first N`, a schema-capped prefix, a query\'s meter-bounded prefix, a list\'s `<=N`, `range`\'s int expressions, `walk`\'s depth,\nor `tokens`\' 64. An accumulated list is bounded by the heap meter and its\niterations by the step meter. `while` stays refused.\n`range(a, b)` evaluates int expressions once and iterates under the step meter,\nwithout materializing the range. `check --cost` prints `steps ≤ meter`, a\nfiltered scan as `filter over ≤ examined ceiling`, its reads as `probe × examined`,\nand a seek as a prefix. Sorting or grouping a page only processes that page.\n`sha256(text)` charges the input\'s UTF-8 byte length plus one to steps before\nhashing, then 64 heap bytes for its fixed lowercase hexadecimal result. A step\ncut refuses a scalar query with E_BOUND; a cut on that result allocation leaves\nthe client read `loading`, as other query heap cuts do. A mutation rolls\nback on either cut.\n\n### Prove the heaviest account\n\nProve the envelope\'s heaviest account, not a typical one: 500 follows and\n1,000 conversations need that account\'s acquisition and cold restart evidence.\nAgainst your running backend, replace alice and heldNotes with your heaviest\npersona and a device query; SB4_URL is dev\'s printed URL, SB4_PROOF a new directory:\n\n```sh\nsnapback4 witness acquire --as alice --url "$SB4_URL" --query heldNotes --restart --artifacts "$SB4_PROOF" --json\n```\n\nFor serve or a real account, use `--as <principal> --token <bearer>`.\nOptional `--args \'{"c":null}\'` supplies query arguments; without `--query` the\nwitness selects the first zero-argument device query. `--runs 3` uses three fresh\nstores. Default URL is http://127.0.0.1:4400; `--timeout 120` bounds each run.\nThe command writes acquire.json even on failure; --json prints it, otherwise\none summary per run. Receipt excerpt (counts depend on your fixture):\n\n```json\n{"passed":true,"runs":[{"coverage":{"groups":1,"rows":2},"restart":{"retained":true,"downloadedRows":0}}]}\n```\n\nIt uses the native process device, the same SQLite module as Expo. Web wasm +\nIndexedDB figures differ. `acquiredMs` ends at the SDK acquired boundary;\n`firstLocalMs` times the first complete local query after that boundary, both\nfrom fresh startup including identity checks. Restart firstLocalMs includes cold\nreopen with all HTTP blocked. Retained means identical store/generation/watermark,\nper-group row counts and persisted coverage, and table row/byte counts before\nreconnect; reconnect must then send no row events (an empty snapshot is possible\nat watermark zero). Keep the fixture\nstable: new writes or expiry can invalidate this comparison. No OS-cache flush,\napp UI, media-cache or physical-phone performance is claimed.\nGroups are table/group pairs with rows or recorded coverage (plus implicit public\nand principal groups); inspect groupsByTable.messages for the conversation count.\nresidency.totalBytes is SQLite pages, excluding WAL/SHM, not RSS; tables give row\npayload bytes. requests preserve Server-Timing and parsed serverMs; serverSyncMs\nsums acquisition sync total durations (null on serve). Verification scans follow\nthe timed answer. Background polling is suppressed; SDK sync itself is unchanged.\nHV\'s measured web guidance on eb1000 (five-run medians, 2026-09-29): 1,000\nmemberships / 105,034 rows acquired in 18.609 s default or 19.522 s Worker;\nfirst inbox 0.441 / 0.520 s, about 307.6 MB allocated wasm memory. These are\nhistorical workload measurements, not a promise or native-device numbers.\n\n### Measuring a growth curve\n\nUse the same backend, query arguments, principal, build and host at each corpus\ncheck'... 16504 more characters,

/tmp/sb4-w11-proof-reviews/codex-r1.txt:2352:snapback4/reports/20260930-na-native-acquisition/round1/affected-sdk.log:596:    actual: '//! The bounds card. Quarry examples are compiled by tests/guide.rs.\n// HV web figures: reports/20260929-hv-heavy-acquisition/REPORT.md, five-run table.\npub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Bounds and the meter\n\nThe meter limits the work each read, mutation and job transaction can do.\nDeclare product limits; the engine chooses execution bounds within those ceilings.\n\n```quarry\ntable scalePosts:\n  body: text <=500\n  public\n  online only\nquery scalePage(c: cursor ?):\n  return scalePosts first 20 by .id after c\nquery scalePopulation():\n  return count(scalePosts)\n```\n\nWrite product limits: a page size, a text length, an asset size or a real per-group cap.\nDo not choose an index, a merge width or a job transaction size. Predicate reads and views choose their plans; top-level job `for`\nloops choose their chunks. Removing a written number never removes the meter.\n\nA query\'s `for row in t where .owner = viewer:` and a merge lane\'s source may omit `first N`\nwhen the read rule is uniformly decided by that prefix; they read ascending\nuntil the prefix ends or the meter cuts, and `check --cost` prints `rows ≤ meter`\nand `lanes ≤ meter` when no fitting schema cap supplies a static bound.\nA loop the meter cuts has no continuation; a total over a group that can outgrow one read is a maintained `view`.\nA returned or bound page instead needs a schema cap or an explicit page bound.\nJobs may iterate uncapped domains under the chunk\'s meter. Top-level job\n`for` sweeps shrink their transaction size after a meter refusal. Mutation loops\nstill need a schema-capped group that fits the examined ceiling. Direct\n`count(domain)`, `sum(domain, .column)` and `exists domain` stream under the\nmeter without retaining the page. Schema-capped query `count` and `sum` with\nnon-uniform read rules keep their bounded page execution and coverage contract.\nMutations retain their uniform-read and plain-prefix admission rules; a cut aborts the transaction, never commits a\npartial total. A streaming query total with a meter cut or unknown device\ncoverage leaves the client read `loading`, never a partial count or sum.\nUnknown earlier reads also make a streaming scalar unavailable, including\ninputs saved in locals or used as domain bounds; a fallback cannot establish\ncoverage.\n\n## Allocation and completeness\n\nHeap is a cumulative allocation and binding budget. Streaming aggregates\nrelease each transient row and its key scratch; one cursor allowance remains.\nInner job domains decode one prepaid row at a time and retain each selected\nrow under the heap meter before fetching another. Once decoded, the row\'s\nretained allowance replaces its decode scratch. Retained rows, their vector\nand the live cursor stay charged; domains do not retain unused result keys.\nConstructed values,\nconcatenation and other allocating builtins, local bindings, and owned\ncopies count. Inspecting a held value\'s length or sum borrows it; row and\nmap clones share their immutable image. A heap cut stops a query immediately\nwith no available value or continuation, since an interrupted scalar or shape\nhas no valid partial answer. The client read is `loading`; narrow the query or\nuse a maintained view for a total. Its cut site remains internal;\nthe meter is available only through dev\'s default read inspection (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §4).\nAn unfinished maintained lookup or live aggregate uses the same unavailable\nquery result, preserving zero only for a completed missing-group lookup.\nA mutation rolls back; Operations below describes the refusal.\nA page whose read rule the prefix does not decide (the deciding fields include a\ncolumn the scan does not pin) is never `complete`: the server cannot tell\n"no more" from "more you may not see". Pin the deciding columns in the predicate, or read the page on the device (`guide sync`), where the\npartition holds exactly what the viewer may read. `check` says so\n(`N_NEVER_COMPLETE`), as it notes a query page returned without a cursor\n(`N_NO_CURSOR`), a synced table whose groups can grow with no horizon\n(`N_NO_HORIZON`), and a merge past 50 lanes (`N_WIDE_MERGE`).\n`complete` means the query answered its declared request within known coverage and execution limits.\nFilling `first N` or `last N` is complete, including a composed preview.\nA cursorless page cannot continue; declare `c: cursor ?` and use `after c`\nif the screen pages. Composed fields and pages covering a fitting group cap\nomit `N_NO_CURSOR`.\n\n## Operations\n\nNothing is priced at check. As a program runs, the meter counts examined\nrows, index entries, rule probes, steps, heap bytes, output bytes and rows\nwritten against ceilings: 8,192 rows examined, 16,384 index entries, 8,192\nrule probes, 1,000,000 steps, 16 MiB heap, 1 MiB output, 1,024 rows written\nper mutation; a job\'s chunk may examine 16,384 rows and write 8,192. A\nquery past a ceiling returns what it found with `complete: false`; a\nmutation aborts with E_BOUND naming the meter, the ceiling and the site\n(never the count: it would measure rows the viewer may not read).\nRead a smaller page, narrow its predicate, or move the sweep into a job.\nAuthorization-dependent scans prepay a fixed logical allowance before row\ndiscovery. Hidden candidates do not consume the caller\'s remaining allowance;\nreadable rows, filters and output still do. These logical meters exclude private\nscan work: authorization candidates share one physical limit of 8,192\nper request, including nested witnesses and maintained-source walks. A row behind at least 8,192 such candidates is unreachable by paging\nin that direction; an empty page has no cursor into the hidden range. Expiring\ncap checks draw from that same allowance; any authorization that cannot be\nestablished within it returns generic E_RULE without identifying a cause.\nThe device\'s prediction-work budget can instead queue an operation.\n`snapback4 check --cost` prints each site\'s structural class;\n`snapback4 test` prints the numbers on your data and names query/persona reads\npast half any ceiling without a cut: at most ten lines and a count of the rest.\nLoopback `dev` logs actual meter and word cuts with the operation, principal,\nsite, counters and `snapback4 why <op>`. Repeats are suppressed per operation\nand site (at most two lines per minute); the next emitted line includes the\nsuppressed count. `serve` neither logs these costs nor returns read meters.\nA loop is bounded by a page\'s `first N`, a schema-capped prefix, a query\'s meter-bounded prefix, a list\'s `<=N`, `range`\'s int expressions, `walk`\'s depth,\nor `tokens`\' 64. An accumulated list is bounded by the heap meter and its\niterations by the step meter. `while` stays refused.\n`range(a, b)` evaluates int expressions once and iterates under the step meter,\nwithout materializing the range. `check --cost` prints `steps ≤ meter`, a\nfiltered scan as `filter over ≤ examined ceiling`, its reads as `probe × examined`,\nand a seek as a prefix. Sorting or grouping only processes that page.\n`sha256(text)` charges the input\'s UTF-8 byte length plus one to steps before\nhashing, then 64 heap bytes for its fixed lowercase hexadecimal result. A step\ncut refuses a scalar query with E_BOUND; a cut on that result allocation leaves\nthe client read `loading`, as other query heap cuts do. A mutation rolls\nback on either cut.\n\n### Prove the heaviest account\n\nProve the envelope\'s heaviest account: 500 follows and 1,000 conversations need acquisition/restart proof.\nReplace alice/heldNotes with that persona/query; SB4_URL is dev\'s URL, SB4_PROOF a new directory:\n\n```sh\nsnapback4 witness acquire --as alice --url "$SB4_URL" --query heldNotes --restart --artifacts "$SB4_PROOF" --json\n```\n\nFor serve or a real account, use `--as <principal> --token <bearer>` (or `--token=<bearer>`). Optional `--args \'{"c":null}\'`\nsupplies query arguments; without `--query` the witness selects the first zero-argument device query. `--runs 3` uses three\nfresh stores. Default URL is http://127.0.0.1:4400; `--timeout 120` bounds each run. The command writes acquire.json even on\nfailure; --json prints it, otherwise one summary per run. Receipt excerpt (counts depend on your fixture):\n\n```json\n{"passed":true,"runs":[{"coverage":{"groups":1,"rows":2},"restart":{"retained":true,"downloadedRows":0}}]}\n```\n\nNode sends one JSON line per call to the SQLite `snapback4 device` subprocess. Expo links the same crate in-process; these are\nnot Expo/iOS or physical-phone timings. `acquiredMs` ends at SDK acquired; `firstLocalMs` ends at the subsequent complete local\nquery. Both start at fresh startup, including identity checks. Restart firstLocalMs includes cold offline reopen;\n`restart.coverageVerifiedMs` separately times coverage scan/comparison. Retained means equal store/generation/watermark,\nper-group counts/coverage and table rows/bytes. Reconnect must finish a validated `more: false` stream at the applied server\nposition, with no row events or failed attempts. Even an empty snapshot at watermark zero needs catch-up. Keep the fixture\nstable: writes/expiry invalidate comparison. OS caches are warm; app UI and media caches are excluded. Verification scans follow\nthe timed answer; background polling is suppressed. Groups are table/group pairs with rows/coverage, plus implicit\npublic/principal groups. groupsByTable and residency.tables hash names with SHA-256 of UTF-8 JSON.stringify(["witness-table",\nname]); use name = "messages" for conversation groups. residency.totalBytes is SQLite pages, excluding WAL/SHM and RSS; table\nbytes are row payloads. Receipt format 2 retains local timings/counts and computed state/position digests; server text, codes,\nnumeric metadata and Server-Timing are dropped. Validated codes select argument-rejection, query-refusal or incomplete-coverage\ndiagnostics: check --args types, --query requirements or held coverage respectively. Failed receipts retain caller query/args.\nAutomatic queries get a generated label; explicit query/args/principal echo only caller input.\n\nHV web medians (eb1000, five r'... 17885 more characters,

/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/3000.007-trending-needs-no-new-authority.md:4:the wave-11 `snapback4` binary. This closes — or at least reframes — the
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:158:| MATERIAL-2 — reserved-path denylist incomplete and itself the drift pattern | **RESOLVED** | D2b reserves by mechanism (dedicated prefix or derivation from `all_route_registrations()`), names the second-literal-list hazard explicitly, and the corrected missed-prefix list is complete against the tree — it includes `/outbox/*`, which my own round-1 sweep missed. |
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.

/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.

/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r5-1_j3bw07/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/0098-hosted-edge-adapter.spec.md:875:  `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` before any write, consuming only the

/tmp/sb4-ref-r8-xi7k63ym/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r8-xi7k63ym/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r8-xi7k63ym/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r8-xi7k63ym/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:161:6. **Minor — checkpoint header staleness and diagnostic registry.** The checkpoint is dated 2026-07-22 (line 34) but records round-1-fix events from 07-23 (e.g., the reflected-nonce gap closure, verified at index.mjs:1951-1958). Also `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` is emitted (src/server.rs:8291) and specified (line 446) but is absent from LLP 0060's reserved sender-diagnostic list (0060 lines 908-910); the reservation there should grow the fourth code when this Spec is accepted.

/tmp/sb4-ref-r8-xi7k63ym/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.

/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.

/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:158:| MATERIAL-2 — reserved-path denylist incomplete and itself the drift pattern | **RESOLVED** | D2b reserves by mechanism (dedicated prefix or derivation from `all_route_registrations()`), names the second-literal-list hazard explicitly, and the corrected missed-prefix list is complete against the tree — it includes `/outbox/*`, which my own round-1 sweep missed. |

/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:161:6. **Minor — checkpoint header staleness and diagnostic registry.** The checkpoint is dated 2026-07-22 (line 34) but records round-1-fix events from 07-23 (e.g., the reflected-nonce gap closure, verified at index.mjs:1951-1958). Also `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` is emitted (src/server.rs:8291) and specified (line 446) but is absent from LLP 0060's reserved sender-diagnostic list (0060 lines 908-910); the reservation there should grow the fourth code when this Spec is accepted.

Neither appears anywhere in `src/`, and LLP 0012:704 explicitly classes both as "reserved hosted/v2 … the local bearer-v1 target must not emit these as implemented capability."
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.
/tmp/sb4-merge-confirm-r5/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:158:| MATERIAL-2 — reserved-path denylist incomplete and itself the drift pattern | **RESOLVED** | D2b reserves by mechanism (dedicated prefix or derivation from `all_route_registrations()`), names the second-literal-list hazard explicitly, and the corrected missed-prefix list is complete against the tree — it includes `/outbox/*`, which my own round-1 sweep missed. |

/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/3000.007-agent-ergonomics-wave-11.claude.md:122:### 5 — High (pre-existing; wave 11 changed no client source). The bounded lock wait is not sufficient: it lets a *live sibling tab* adopt a just-closed tab's store and destroy the receipt the reloading tab needs
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r7-mc1o411w/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.

/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:161:6. **Minor — checkpoint header staleness and diagnostic registry.** The checkpoint is dated 2026-07-22 (line 34) but records round-1-fix events from 07-23 (e.g., the reflected-nonce gap closure, verified at index.mjs:1951-1958). Also `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` is emitted (src/server.rs:8291) and specified (line 446) but is absent from LLP 0060's reserved sender-diagnostic list (0060 lines 908-910); the reservation there should grow the fourth code when this Spec is accepted.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.

/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/union-r7-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
