/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/sb4-ref-r6-3kovdt_b/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).

The Miniflare loopback proof is validated only on Node 24.x, pinned to v24.18.0 in .node-version (ENG-24513). On unsupported majors the first real-loopback action request intermittently fails with `read ECONNRESET` (observed on Node 25.9.0, ENG-24454) — a Miniflare/Node transport-lifecycle race, not an action-semantic failure. This preflight constrains the supported runtime; it does not fix the Node 25 transport (upstream-shaped) and no action retry hides it. /tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:161:6. **Minor — checkpoint header staleness and diagnostic registry.** The checkpoint is dated 2026-07-22 (line 34) but records round-1-fix events from 07-23 (e.g., the reflected-nonce gap closure, verified at index.mjs:1951-1958). Also `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` is emitted (src/server.rs:8291) and specified (line 446) but is absent from LLP 0060's reserved sender-diagnostic list (0060 lines 908-910); the reservation there should grow the fourth code when this Spec is accepted.

/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.

/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0060-hosted-auth-security.fable.md:271:Evidence: §Diagnostics marks the three `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes "reserved for the v2 protocol," but lists `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SOURCE_UNTRUSTED` and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_INVALIDATION_LAGGING` unqualified. Neither appears anywhere in `src/`, and LLP 0012:704 explicitly classes both as "reserved hosted/v2 … the local bearer-v1 target must not emit these as implemented capability."
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/union-r5-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.

The Miniflare loopback proof is validated only on Node 24.x, pinned to v24.18.0 in .node-version (ENG-24513). On unsupported majors the first real-loopback action request intermittently fails with `read ECONNRESET` (observed on Node 25.9.0, ENG-24454) — a Miniflare/Node transport-lifecycle race, not an action-semantic failure. This preflight constrains the supported runtime; it does not fix the Node 25 transport (upstream-shaped) and no action retry hides it. Switch to the pinned Node before running the proof, e.g. `volta install node@24.18.0`, `fnm use`, or `nvm install 24.18.0 && nvm use` (each reads .node-version).

/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:161:6. **Minor — checkpoint header staleness and diagnostic registry.** The checkpoint is dated 2026-07-22 (line 34) but records round-1-fix events from 07-23 (e.g., the reflected-nonce gap closure, verified at index.mjs:1951-1958). Also `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` is emitted (src/server.rs:8291) and specified (line 446) but is absent from LLP 0060's reserved sender-diagnostic list (0060 lines 908-910); the reservation there should grow the fourth code when this Spec is accepted.

/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.

/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:51:§D2 constraints. The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.
/tmp/union-r6-review/llp/reviews/0141-embeddable-rust-library.fable.md:158:| MATERIAL-2 — reserved-path denylist incomplete and itself the drift pattern | **RESOLVED** | D2b reserves by mechanism (dedicated prefix or derivation from `all_route_registrations()`), names the second-literal-list hazard explicitly, and the corrected missed-prefix list is complete against the tree — it includes `/outbox/*`, which my own round-1 sweep missed. |

The listed reservations (`/_snapback/*`, `/auth/*`, `/live/*`, `/query/*`, `/mutation/*`, "the admin surface") omit real first-party namespaces I enumerated from the registration builders: `/action/*`, `/app/*` (`/app/ensure`, `/app/bundle`), `/assets/*`, `/connection/*`, `/client-mutations/*`, `/ephemeral/*`, `/health`, `/inspect`, and `/`. Worse, a hand-maintained reserved-prefix list is precisely "a second route-literal list that can drift outside policy" — the exact hazard the RFC quotes approvingly from `app()`'s doc comment. The reservation should be derived from `all_route_registrations()` (or inverted: extensions confined to a dedicated prefix), and the RFC should say so.

/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:23:This is a strong, unusually disciplined piece of security engineering. The design lands squarely inside the slice LLP 0060 C3 delegates: every item C3 owes before a positive capability — issuance vs. resource proofs (with `ath` present only on resource proofs), explicit inclusion/exclusion of every credential class, HTTP and WebSocket transport, canonical URI reconstruction behind the provider-authenticated target (src/auth/deployment.rs:367–391), a closed ES256-only JOSE profile, time-window and nonce policy, replay-store dimensions and fail-closed behavior, downgrade refusal, and key persistence/deletion/recovery — is covered by a section of this Spec. The claim discipline C3 pins ("off-device replay constraint, never XSS-resistant") is restated correctly (llp 0077 lines 109–111, 302–304), and `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_*` codes match LLP 0060's reservation (0060 lines 907–910) plus one new `AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` code that fits the fail-closed posture.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:42:2. **Moderate — Diagnostics section contradicts the nonce protocol and the code.** "They expose no distinction between unknown token, wrong key, bad signature, wrong nonce, stale time, or wrong target" (llp lines 276–277) is false as written: wrong/retired nonce yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a fresh `DPoP-Nonce`, distinct from `INVALID` and `REPLAYED` (sender_proof.rs:858–870; server.rs:8277–8293), and the nonce section itself mandates that distinction (llp lines 174–177). The nonce carve-out is necessary (it is RFC 9449's `use_dpop_nonce` pattern); the normative sentence just needs to scope the non-distinction to within `INVALID`.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.fable.md:157:2. **Moderate — the `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence rule is contradicted by a committed test.** Lines 240-244 say an absent or unparseable bearer yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, "not a sender-proof diagnostic". The pinned test sends `Bearer malformed-session-carrier` (unparseable as a session token; `SessionBearer::parse` fails, family `None`) and asserts `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` plus a 43-char `DPoP-Nonce` (src/server.rs:23667-23696; code path src/server.rs:8391-8406 → sender_proof.rs:757-773). Either the doc means only an absent/malformed Authorization *header* (then say so — the anti-oracle rationale at lines 292-294 supports challenging format-invalid tokens), or the code and test are wrong and this belongs in the defect ledger. Right now a conformance tester cannot tell which artifact is authoritative.

/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:140:7. **Major — Admission precedence conflicts with LLP 0057/0058 without an explicit amendment.** LLP 0077 maps unknown tokens into `SNAPBACK_AUTH_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:395-399`), whereas LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require malformed or unknown bearers to produce `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). LLP 0077 also consumes sender proof before body ingestion (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:214-223`), while LLP 0058 requires forbidden authority fields in the body to win before considering a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1072-1088,1157-1168`). Resolve this with an exhaustive token-state × proof-state × nonce-state × body-state precedence table and explicit amendments to LLP 0057/0058.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:229:2. **Major — There is no coherent normative request-precedence algorithm.** The Summary says proof verification precedes reading an application body (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:163-167`), while the normative order places LLP 0058’s forbidden-body-field inspection before proof parsing and consumption (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:228-239`). LLP 0058 requires that forbidden field to win even beside a valid bearer (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1112-1116`). The server currently performs sender admission first and rejects forbidden fields later (`src/server.rs:8497-8519`, `8853-8862`). Credential precedence is also contradictory: LLP 0077 requires absent/unparseable carriers to yield `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:241-244`) but later includes an unknown token under `_SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:448-452`), contrary to LLP 0057 (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:309-313`). The served test currently asserts malformed bearer → `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` without a proof and `_INVALID` with one (`src/server.rs:23667-23721`). Resolve with one explicit two-stage state machine defining body polling, bearer classification, proof verification, replay/nonce mutation, and diagnostic precedence, plus combined negative tests asserting both result and unchanged replay/nonce state.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:433:4. **Major — Diagnostic precedence conflicts with Accepted authorities and the code implements neither contract consistently.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while claiming this “composes” with LLP 0057 (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:298-306`). Accepted LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 require `AUTH_REQUIRED` for malformed or unknown bearers (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`); their recorded amendments do not delegate this diagnostic change. Meanwhile, the server converts an absent required header to an empty bearer and invokes sender admission (`src/server.rs:8391-8423`), which returns `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` when no proof is present (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-776`), contrary to LLP 0077’s own absent-header rule. With an unknown carrier and a supplied proof, it instead returns `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` without the promised cover nonce (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Resolve by choosing one complete carrier/proof diagnostic matrix, explicitly amending LLP 0057/0058 if necessary, and adding served pre-body tests for every matrix cell.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:437:6. **Major — A lost logout response can strand valid-looking local custody indefinitely.** The generic lost-response rule assumes the family remains resolvable so the server can return its current nonce (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:371-381`), but successful logout revokes that family. Sender lookup only joins current, unrevoked sessions (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:1107-1126`); the client clears custody only after receiving the logout response (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:3106-3144`), and its same-stream `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` behavior deliberately preserves v3 custody (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-436`). Without a live durable-delete signal, the next signed request cannot obtain a usable nonce or prove anonymous succession. Resolve with a bounded revoked-family sender tombstone sufficient to authenticate and return terminal `durableDelete`, or a specified anonymous recovery handshake that safely proves the terminal outcome before clearing custody; add a committed-logout/transport-loss test.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:532:6. **Major — diagnostic precedence contradicts accepted authorities and current code.** LLP 0077 says malformed or unknown present carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` while a genuinely absent header receives `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:331-339`). LLP 0057 and LLP 0058 both assign malformed and unknown carriers `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:767-775,1098-1105`; `llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1121-1125`). Current required-route code also converts an absent header to an empty bearer and issues the sender-proof challenge (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-776`). Add an explicit authority amendment or retain existing precedence, then pin statuses, response schema, `WWW-Authenticate`, `DPoP-Nonce`, and CORS exposure. RFC 9449’s nonce pattern uses 401 plus both `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP ...` and `DPoP-Nonce` ([RFC 9449 §9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449.html#section-9)).
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:621:   The HTTP section says absent `Authorization` yields `SNAPBACK_AUTH_REQUIRED`, while malformed or unknown carriers receive `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:359-367`). The nonce section instead includes an absent carrier among cases receiving an uninstalled sender challenge (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:415-423`). Both differ from LLP 0057 and LLP 0059, which require structurally invalid and unknown bearers to remain uniformly `AUTH_REQUIRED` (`llp/0057-auth-provider-protocol.spec.md:762-775`, `llp/0059-session-projection-and-client-barrier.spec.md:2585-2593`).
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:623:   The shipped behavior matches neither complete story: required routes convert absent and malformed carriers into an empty bearer (`src/server.rs:8391-8406`); no proof then produces `SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, while a proof with no family produces `SENDER_PROOF_INVALID` (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-777`). The client’s reset recovery probe runs only after `_INVALID`, not `_REQUIRED` (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:2161-2180`), and the reset test mocks that shipped `_INVALID` divergence (`packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:175-185`, `packages/snapback-client/sender-transport.test.mjs:428-452`). A conforming `_REQUIRED` server would therefore exhaust the one retry and strand custody (`packages/snapback-client/index.mjs:1994-2044`).
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:691:   Resolve this with exact schema validation during hosted preflight and a negative test proving that a weakened or incompatible table produces `SENDER_PROOF_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
/tmp/claude-1000/-home-ccheever-projects-snapback/fdf7fb80-9b69-48f2-b659-1b99e033e6e3/scratchpad/review-r5/llp/reviews/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.codex.md:890:6. **Major — The pre-body authority order is contradictory and not implemented.** LLP 0058’s revision note says sender admission precedes body inspection (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:8-16`), but its normative gate still places forbidden-body inspection before credential consideration (`llp/0058-shared-invocation-authority-and-staged-auth-mode-protocol.spec.md:1081-1097,1121-1125`). The server omits the required D3 shared-throttle charge before proof processing (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:377-393`; `src/server.rs:8491-8516`), resolves `SessionAuth` before the forbidden-field check (`src/server.rs:8533-8588,8853-8861`), and performs family lookup before exact compact-proof decoding (`src/auth/sender_proof.rs:757-790,1234-1285`). Additionally, genuinely absent Authorization on a required hosted route is converted to an empty carrier and produces `_SENDER_PROOF_REQUIRED`, contradicting the specified `AUTH_REQUIRED` precedence (`llp/0077-sender-constrained-browser-auth.spec.md:412-420,446-464`; `src/server.rs:8391-8406`; `src/auth/sender_proof.rs:765-773`). Resolve this by amending LLP 0058’s normative algorithm—not only its metadata—and implementing one tested stage machine for throttle, proof decoding, carrier classification, family lookup, body-field rejection, and credential projection.

## 10. Wave 10 release lifecycle amendment

Commission: Charlie's REL2 workstream, 2026-10-13; implementer Codex in
sb4-w10-rel2. This amends §9.2's definition of a complete release and §2's
capture restoration. No new language, planner or device protocol is introduced.

A newly packaged numeric source directory carries `.pending-adoption`, written
and fsynced before publication. It is selectable only if database activation
history proves that its adoption committed. Successful deploy removes the marker;
a crash, refused adoption or lost control reply leaves it. `latest`, numeric
rollback, release inspection and check-release all use this rule. The database
proof recovers a committed activation even if pointer/ledger completion failed,
and recognizes its committed predecessor as proof of legacy activation.
For retained stores, committed history gates unmarked directories too, excluding
refused candidates from older binaries. Legacy stores without release history
keep their complete-source selection rule. The marker is excluded from the
source digest. All production writes of numeric
release source go through deploy; rollback and serve only select existing source.
A refused candidate remains a reserved identity and can never become a selectable
release merely because it compiles. A matching-source dormant-name refusal leads
with `rollback <dir> --to latest`; other source uses `--restore-dormant`. Human
retention evidence lists counts/samples rather than serialized inventory JSON.

Bare rollback selects the previous release of the last committed activation,
including rollback and roll-forward; prune and same-release selections are excluded. Thus v1 → v2 → v3 → v1
followed by bare rollback activates v3. An explicit --to overrides this. Legacy
stores without activation history require explicit --to. A no-op adds no event.

`deploy --check` runs the exact candidate activation on the private snapshot with
**deploy** restoration intent, while rollback/check-release previews select
restoration. No candidate is selectable and live state remains unchanged.
Preview validates static worker prerequisites without starting workers. Successful
constraint narration comes from the validated candidate after dormant projection,
not the predecessor population. Successful previews describe dormant rows/cells with counts and bounded ids,
report successful maintain rebuild source visits, and omit speculative adoption
failures and capture-fill promises. Compatibility warnings are advisory, only for
populated affected tables, and include their current row count (not a prediction
that every row is incompatible). Preview flags removed schedules and populated
removed application tables as business safeguards absent from the target. Quarry
has no hold declaration: every removed populated table gets this advisory, so a
hold's name cannot evade it. Existing live writes can change any preview result.

Retired engine capture columns retain their source table definition alongside
cells. Restoring the same read-through definition uses the preserved capture and
parent lifetime, even when the intervening release widened its read rule. The
predecessor audience correspondence barrier applies only to rows requiring new
captures. Historical rows must still pass retained reference lifetime and
capture/parent validation. A changed retained read-through definition refuses;
new rows without historical captures retain the existing first-adoption checks.
Source expiry is evaluated before projection, so restoration cannot revive an
expired captured lifetime. Recorded attribution keeps its historical value.

`run <job> --release <dir>` routes to the served store at its canonical loopback
address using `SNAPBACK4_SERVICE_TOKEN` or `--token-env NAME`; it needs no local
project/source. `POST /jobs/<job>` requires a full service capability, rechecked
under the enqueueing engine lock, and executes system work on the existing FIFO.
Every full service seat is privileged to trigger any system job; operators must
provision these tokens accordingly. Accounts, guests, read scopes and personas
are refused. `/dev/jobs` stays development-only. The reply waits for done/parked;
a lost reply is ambiguous and does not authorize automatic retry. It adds no job
priority, worker pool, token scope or idempotency contract.

`serve.lock` remains an address hint. Only successful sidecar ownership allows
reclamation. After acquiring it, an ESRCH result from kill(pid,0) identifies a dead
record and emits a diagnostic with PID, URL and directory before overwriting it.
A live/reused PID or uncertain probe never authorizes signalling or lock bypass.

Acceptance: v1/v2/rollback/refused-v3/latest and explicit refused selection;
non-numeric-history rollback; deploy preview state equality; empty/populated
warnings; all packaged rollback claims; old release E_OP/NOT_FOUND/null and exact
forward values; retained captures after widening plus deleted/reused parent and
new-row refusal; service-token job completion and unauthorized/read-only requests;
stale-lock recovery and live-owner conflict provenance. HS owns handshake/types;
DX2 owns starters/client recipes; DIAG and PAGE2 own language/planner diagnostics.


REL2 review round one: fresh GPT-6.1-sol found legacy predecessor selection,
pre-dormancy constraint narration and omitted static worker prerequisite checks.
All three were reproduced and corrected with migration, unique/narrowing and
worker-selection regressions. Review artifacts retain the verbatim findings.


REL2 round two: [Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.muse.md) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-rel2-wave10-r2.codex.md) both READY on
this amendment and implementation. Focused final verification passes. Informational
receipt sampling also covers populated items beyond empty inventory definitions;
guide consistency lives in its own test module, preserving the pre-existing
parent test file's line count. No publication or push was performed by REL2.

//! Immutable source releases with a database-authoritative activation pointer.
use super::*;
use crate::store_owner::Owner;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
struct Stage(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Stage {fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}

fn directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    options
        .positionals
        .first()
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .ok_or_else(|| "a deployment directory is required".into())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5.1 — deployment aliases share ownership, control and worker paths.
fn canonical_directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let requested = directory(options)?;
    if !requested.exists() {
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&requested).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&dir).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(dir)
}

/// `dev` and `serve` hold the store-owner lock for their lifetime. Release
/// commands route to the admitted server when it owns this deployment.
pub(crate) fn deployment_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<Owner, String> {
    crate::store_owner::command(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))
}
fn current(dir: &Path, owner: &Owner) -> Result<Option<u64>, String> {
    let database = dir.join("store.sqlite");
    if database.exists() {
        let store = owner
            .open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if let Some(release) = store.meta("release").map_err(|e| e.to_string())? {
            return release
                .parse()
                .map(Some)
                .map_err(|_| "invalid release identity in store".into());
        }
    }
    // Compatibility with deployments made before the database owned this
    // pointer. The next activation records it transactionally.
    match std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")) {
        Ok(text) => text
            .trim()
            .parse()
            .map(Some)
            .map_err(|_| "invalid current release file".into()),
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
        Err(e) => Err(format!("cannot read current release: {e}")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn bound_at(root: PathBuf) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, String> {
    let project = Project { root };
    if project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?.is_empty() {
        return Err("release has no Quarry source; select a complete immutable release".into());
    }
    let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        return Err(bound
            .diagnostics
            .iter()
            .map(ToString::to_string)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>()
            .join("\n"));
    }
    Ok(bound)
}
fn check_change(
    dir: &Path,
    previous: Option<u64>,
    bound: &snapback4_lang::Bound,
    options: &Options,
) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(n) = previous {
        let old = bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(n.to_string()))?;
        let mut hazards=std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        for step in snapback4_core::successor::classify(&bound.schema,&old.schema) {
            if step.change==snapback4_core::successor::Change::Narrowing && step.path.contains(".columns.") {
                hazards.insert(step.path.replace("tables.","").replace(".columns.","."));
            }
        }
        for (name,current) in &old.schema.tables {
            let Some(candidate)=bound.schema.table(name) else {continue};
            for constraint in &current.constraints {
                if let snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field,min,format}=constraint {
                    if !candidate.columns.contains_key(field) {continue;}
                    let wanted=candidate.constraints.iter().find_map(|c|match c {
                        snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field:f,min,format} if f==field =>Some((*min,*format)),_=>None,
                    }).unwrap_or((0,None));
                    if wanted.0<*min || format.is_some() && wanted.1!=*format {hazards.insert(format!("{name}.{field}"));}
                }
            }
        }
        let rows = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite")).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
        for path in hazards {
            let table=path.split('.').next().unwrap();
            let count: u64=rows.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM r WHERE t=(SELECT num FROM _catalog WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,i64>(0).map(|n|n as u64)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            if count == 0 {continue;}
            eprintln!("WARNING rollback compatibility: {path} ({count} existing rows) admits values the other release refuses; those rows become dormant when switching back");
        }
    }
    if options.flags.contains_key("allow-destructive") {
        eprintln!(
            "deploy/rollback retain storage; --allow-destructive does not bypass validation or prune. To drop dormant storage: snapback4 prune {} --allow-destructive",
            dir.display()
        );
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn command_lock(dir: &Path) -> Result<std::fs::File, String> {
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        let file = snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(
            &dir.join("store.sqlite"),
            &dir.join(".release-command"),
            true,
            0,
        )
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        file.lock().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        Ok(file)
    }
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    Err("release commands require Unix".into())
}
fn target(dir: &Path) -> Result<Option<Owner>, String> {
    match deployment_lock(dir) {
        Ok(owner) => Ok(Some(owner)),
        Err(error) if error.contains("E_STORE_BUSY") => Ok(None),
        Err(error) => Err(error),
    }
}
fn status(
    dir: &Path,

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# LLP 3000.011.003.000.000: Packaged application proof kit
     2	
     3	**Type:** RFC
     4	**Status:** Review
     5	**Systems:** Snapback 4 proof tooling, starters, fixtures, release source selection
     6	**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever
     7	**Date:** 2026-10-14
     8	**Revised:** 2026-10-14
     9	**Related:** [Starters](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md), [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Built application proof](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md)
    10	
    11	## 1. Contract
    12	
    13	Implementer: PROOF workstream, 2026-10-14. Wave 11 fix list 1 authorizes
    14	one published `snapback4/proof` Node module. It owns orchestration, never client
    15	custody or backend/bundler supervision in the product. Existing `startDev(args)`
    16	and `stopChild(child | {url, stop})` recipes remain usable as module exports.
    17	
    18	`startDev`, `startServe(directory)`, `startStatic(directory)` and
    19	`startVite(directory)` return `{url, pid, logs, stop}`. Options carry cwd,
    20	environment, arguments and bounded ready/stop deadlines. Read PORT_LO/PORT_HI
    21	at invocation; require both, validate their inclusive range, default to an
    22	OS-assigned port. The listening process binds first and keeps that socket;
    23	never adopt an already-serving port. Vite uses its supported JavaScript server
    24	API in a directly spawned Node child, strict ports, the project's config and
    25	a per-launch identity middleware. Static hosting uses a directly spawned Node
    26	child with traversal protection, correct wasm MIME and SPA fallback.
    27	
    28	All child stdout/stderr go to separate files beneath `logs/`. Read complete
    29	lines from those files, strip ANSI, bound readiness, and reject premature exit.
    30	Native ready lines must name the directly spawned PID. To assert HTTP identity,
    31	the kit sets a random SNAPBACK4_PROOF_ID only in the child environment;
    32	`/auth/config` includes `proof_id` only when this variable is nonempty. It
    33	confers no authority or bypass, and ordinary servers omit it. Frontend child
    34	responses similarly carry an `x-snapback4-proof` marker. Identity validation
    35	precedes returning a handle. No public development door is enabled in serve.
    36	
    37	Register every spawned child and browser process for an exit-time SIGKILL
    38	sweep, using retained native ChildProcess handles, not process-name kills.
    39	TERM followed by bounded KILL/reap stops a child. Every cleanup action has
    40	its own bound and later actions still run when an earlier action fails.
    41	`cli(args)` runs the native binary, awaits close, returns separate stdout and
    42	stderr, and has a command deadline. It never runs npm/npx or shell strings.
    43	
    44	`withBrowser({executablePath: process.env.CHROME}, callback)` launches Chromium
    45	through playwright-core, closes with a bound, and registers its actual browser
    46	process. Root needs --no-sandbox; other hosts first attempt a sandboxed launch
    47	and retry without it only on recognized sandbox-unavailable diagnostics.
    48	`waitForFresh` polls for nonempty changed text; `fillRN` clears and types real
    49	keystrokes; `statusText` uses text selectors; `selectByValue` selects stable
    50	option values. None interprets an HTTP acknowledgement as rendered settlement.
    51	
    52	## 2. Explicit source and aliases
    53	
    54	`deploy dir --schema file` compiles exactly that saved Quarry file and stages
    55	those same bytes, without swapping the working schema or generating its types.
    56	It does not concatenate other snapback/*.q files. The normal deployment path
    57	retains multi-file behavior and activation semantics. Server/native companion
    58	packaging uses the current project when one exists; a standalone saved file
    59	has no companions. Keep saved variants outside snapback/ to avoid normal
    60	multi-file compilation. Rollback selects an existing immutable release, never
    61	reads the current schema. The proof `deploy` helper requires a schema path;
    62	`rollback` requires an explicit target; `testSource` requires a source path.
    63	`test --source file` uses the selected bytes in a memory engine (optionally
    64	with fixtures), so it cannot adopt a working store. Diagnostics name the file.
    65	
    66	Saved aliases remain explicit `$name` / `$name.field`. A bare string matching
    67	an earlier save alias refuses with the alias name and `$name` rewrite, before
    68	wire decoding; ordinary strings remain literals. This avoids ambiguous auto
    69	substitution into text fields. Future and misspelled `$name` refuse by name.
    70	Fixture preflight/sweep excludes explicitly saved arguments as before.
    71	
    72	## 3. Starter and qualification
    73	
    74	Web/Expo init emits frontend:proof on the kit and declares playwright-core.
    75	The proof starts a memory dev and built static output, passes the actual URL
    76	as ?api=, signs in through the generated screen and drives a visible form when
    77	the default todos schema is present. The fresh terminal receipt precedes the
    78	rendered row assertion. Adapt installs missing proof infrastructure and keeps
    79	application-owned proof files/scripts. Runtime ranges are never baked from
    80	the initializer's environment; explicit init --port remains a deliberate
    81	client/interactive default, separate from the kit's runtime URL.
    82	
    83	Linux publication qualification runs both generated proofs twice, with an
    84	occupied first port and bounded CPU load. Retain all four exits, log paths and
    85	runtime ranges in its receipt; any failed attempt fails qualification. Test
    86	occupied/exhausted ranges, wrong identities, ANSI/blank ready lines, early death,
    87	command close/drained output, browser flags, cleanup escalation, exit sweeps,
    88	saved-source isolation and alias refusals. Three copied wave-11 proofs retain
    89	their application assertions while replacing orchestration; record line counts
    90	and execution results. Guide testing replaces orchestration recipes with the
    91	kit and four rules: file logs; fresh signals; receipts outside unmounting
    92	components; HTTP acknowledgement is not React settlement.
    93	
    94	NARR owns deploy/rollback narration; DX3 owns general client recipes and discovery.
                None => out.push(format!("{} is the program's own refusal at {shown}", refusal.code)),
            }
        },
    }
    out

/// `op:N` (line N of an operation, its header line 1) as `file:line:1`.
fn operation_site(bound: &Bound, site: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let (op, line) = site.rsplit_once(':')?;
    let line: u32 = line.parse().ok()?;
    let program = bound.programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == op)?;
    Some(format!("{}:{}:1", program.site.file, program.site.line + line.checked_sub(1)?))
}

/// `"$name"` is a saved answer (a saved row's id); `"$name.field"` (any depth) one of its fields.
fn substitute(value: Json, saved: &BTreeMap<String, Json>) -> Result<Json, String> {
    Ok(match value {
        Json::String(text) if text.starts_with('$') && text.len() > 1 => {
            let mut parts = text[1..].split('.');
            let name = parts.next().unwrap_or_default();
            let mut current = saved.get(name).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: nothing was saved as {name} (add \"save\": \"{name}\" to an earlier case)"))?;
            let mut fields = 0;
            for part in parts {
                current = current.get(part).ok_or_else(|| format!("{text}: the saved answer has no field {part}"))?;
                fields += 1;
            }
            // An argument names a row by its id: a bare `$name` for a saved
            // row (an insert's answer) means that row's id.
            match current.get("id").and_then(Json::as_str) { Some(id) if fields == 0 => Json::String(id.to_owned()), _ => current.clone() }
        }
        Json::Array(items) => Json::Array(items.into_iter().map(|item| substitute(item, saved)).collect::<Result<_, _>>()?),
        Json::Object(fields) => Json::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(k, v)| Ok((k, substitute(v, saved)?))).collect::<Result<_, String>>()?),
        other => other,
    })
}

pub fn deploy(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    report(
        "deploy",
        (|| {
            let dir = canonical_directory(options)?;
            let _command = command_lock(&dir)?;
            let checking=options.flags.contains_key("check");
            let _deployment = if checking {None} else {target(&dir)?};
            let project = project_or_exit();
            let bound = compile_or_exit(&project, options.json);
            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("releases")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let held = if checking {
                if dir.join("store.sqlite").exists() {snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&dir.join("store.sqlite")).and_then(|mut s|s.release_status(0,None,None)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?}
                else {serde_json::json!({"release":null,"revision":0,"generation":1})}
            } else {status(&dir, _deployment.as_ref(), 0, None, None)?};
            let previous = if let Some(owner) = _deployment.as_ref() {
                current(&dir, owner)?
            } else {
                held["release"].as_u64()
            };
            let revision = held["revision"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0);
            check_change(&dir, previous, &bound, options)?;
            let sources = project.sources().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let releases = dir.join("releases").canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let stage = releases.join(format!(".stage-{}", std::process::id()));
            if stage.exists() {std::fs::remove_dir_all(&stage).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;}
            std::fs::create_dir(&stage).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let _cleanup=Stage(stage.clone());
            std::fs::create_dir(stage.join("snapback")).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            for (name,text) in sources {
                let path=stage.join("snapback").join(name);
                if let Some(parent)=path.parent(){std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;}
                std::fs::write(path,text).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
            }
            // A native job's module is built from the project and travels with
            // the release beside its source (LLP 3000.005).
            if let Some(built) =
                crate::native::build(&project, &bound).map_err(|p| p.to_string())?
            {
                let generated = stage.join("snapback").join("generated");
                std::fs::create_dir_all(&generated).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                std::fs::copy(&built.path, generated.join("native.wasm"))
                    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            }
            std::fs::write(stage.join("device-compatibility.json"), serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&snapback4_core::device_protocol::compatibility(&bound.schema.public_projection())).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            crate::dev_worker::package(&project, &stage)?;
            if let Some(previous)=previous {
                if held["release"].as_u64()==Some(previous) && crate::release_files::digest(&stage)?==crate::release_files::digest(&releases.join(previous.to_string()))? {
                    std::fs::remove_dir_all(&stage).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
                    if options.json {println!("{}",serde_json::json!({"unchanged":true,"release":previous,"generation":held["generation"]}));}
                    else {println!("unchanged; release {previous} stays current as generation {}",held["generation"]);}
                    return Ok(());
                }
            }

            let staged_bound = bound_at(stage.clone())?;
