/// Snapshot only regular files reached through the project's pinned directories.
/// Dependencies are installed by the host.
pub(crate) fn package(project: &Project, stage: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
    collect(project, Some(stage), false)?;
    // @ref LLP 3000.001.000#workers — immutable source records its host app installation separately.
    std::fs::write(stage.join("worker-package.json"), serde_json::to_vec(&serde_json::json!({ "directory": project.root.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())? })).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?).map_err(|e| e.to_string())

}

/// A launch owns these copied bytes until its process group has stopped.
/// Module dependencies remain installed host code, outside source custody.
// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — validation and execution share one private snapshot.
pub(super) struct Snapshot { root: PathBuf, pub entry: PathBuf }
impl Snapshot {
    pub fn new(project: &Project, name: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
        Self::create(project, name).map_err(|e| format!("workers: {e}"))
    }
    fn create(project: &Project, name: &str) -> io::Result<Self> {
        let (mut owned, stage) = Self::scope(project)?;
        let mut entries = collect(project, Some(&stage), true).map_err(io::Error::other)?;
        owned.entry = entries.remove(name).ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other(format!("worker {name}: declaration is absent")))?;
        Ok(owned)
    }

    pub fn automatic(project: &Project, source: &Path) -> Result<Self, String> {
        Self::copy_automatic(project, source).map_err(|e| format!("workers: {e}"))
    }
    fn copy_automatic(project: &Project, source: &Path) -> io::Result<Self> {
        let (mut owned, stage) = Self::scope(project)?;
        let project_root = project.root.canonicalize()?;
        let root = File::open(&project_root)?;
        let schema = open(&root, std::ffi::OsStr::new("snapback"))?;
        let schema_stage = stage.join("snapback");
        walk(&schema, Path::new(""), Some((&project_root.join("snapback"), &schema_stage)), &mut |path, source| {
            let target = schema_stage.join(path);
            std::fs::create_dir_all(target.parent().unwrap())?;
            // scope copied the schema's package type; use the pinned copy here.
            if path == Path::new("package.json") { std::fs::remove_file(&target)?; }
            let mut output = File::options().write(true).create_new(true).open(target)?;
            io::copy(source, &mut output)?;
            output.set_permissions(source.metadata()?.permissions())?;
            Ok(())
        })?;
        link_package_scopes(&Self::package_root(project)?.join("snapback"), &schema_stage)?;
        Self::checked_imports(&root, &project_root, &stage)?;
        owned.entry = schema_stage.join(source.file_name().ok_or_else(|| io::Error::other("worker entry has no filename"))?);
        if !owned.entry.is_file() { return Err(io::Error::other("automatic worker entry is absent")); }
        Ok(owned)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — checked project imports share source custody with the entry.
    fn checked_imports(root: &File, project_root: &Path, stage: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
        // Read the already pinned copy of the checker's existing dependency
        // graph. It includes missing lookup candidates and host packages too;
        // only present files inside this project belong in the source snapshot.
        let graph = match std::fs::read(stage.join("snapback/generated/server-watch.json")) {
            Ok(bytes) => bytes,
            Err(error) if error.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(()),
            Err(error) => return Err(error),
        };
        type Dependency = (PathBuf, Option<(String, String)>);
        let dependencies: Vec<Dependency> = serde_json::from_slice(&graph).map_err(io::Error::other)?;
        for (path, state) in dependencies {
            // The checker records paths as it resolved them, possibly through a
            // symlinked ancestor (macOS /var is /private/var). Canonicalize only
            // the parent: the final component is still opened without following.
            let located = path.parent().zip(path.file_name())
                .and_then(|(parent, name)| parent.canonicalize().ok().map(|parent| parent.join(name)))
                .unwrap_or_else(|| path.clone());
            let Ok(relative) = located.strip_prefix(project_root) else { continue; };
            if state.is_none() || relative.starts_with("snapback") || relative.components().any(|part| part.as_os_str() == "node_modules") { continue; }
            let mut source = root.try_clone()?;
            for component in relative.components() {
                let std::path::Component::Normal(name) = component else { return Err(io::Error::other("checked worker imports must stay inside the project")); };
                source = open(&source, name)?;
            }
            if !source.metadata()?.is_file() { return Err(io::Error::other("checked worker imports must be regular files")); }
            let target = stage.join(relative);
            std::fs::create_dir_all(target.parent().unwrap())?;
            // scope() may have copied the project's package type. Replace it
            // with these descriptor-validated bytes, not another pathname read.
            if target.exists() { std::fs::remove_file(&target)?; }
            let mut output = File::options().write(true).create_new(true).open(target)?;
            io::copy(&mut source, &mut output)?;
            output.set_permissions(source.metadata()?.permissions())?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn package_root(project: &Project) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
        if let Some(path) = std::env::var_os("SNAPBACK_WORKER_PACKAGE_DIR") { return PathBuf::from(path).canonicalize(); }
        match std::fs::read(project.root.join("worker-package.json")) {

# LLP 3000.011.003: Offline capability still leaves application traps

**Type:** Research
**Status:** Active
**Systems:** Snapback 4 local clients, application navigation, shell caching, acquisition, pending writes
**Author:** Codex / GPT-6 (for Charlie Cheever)
**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Revised:** 2026-09-22 (selected implementation and deferred alternatives reconciled)
**Related:** [Parent](./3000.011-recent-builder-friction.research.md), [Improvement proposal](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md), [LLP 3000.008.001](./3000.008.001-understandable-data-placement.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.008.002](./3000.008.002-complete-client-experience.rfc.md)

## 1. Problem

A durable replica and outbox do not by themselves produce an application that
opens and behaves correctly offline. Routes can require online work before
showing cached data; a browser can lack its shell; a pending write can have no
predicted row; a retained page can be useful without covering all history.
The application must compose those boundaries deliberately.

## 2. Evidence

The [0.2.30 M6 diary](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-18-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.30/astra/lane-diary.md)
records a DM route that ran an online pair-deduplication mutation before
opening an already cached room. A membership lookup fixed startup; the backend
had retained the data correctly. Its essay also describes separately adding
a same-origin service worker for the app shell.

The [0.2.29 M2 diary](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-18-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.29/astra/lane-diary.md)
found a stale Following projection after a nonpredicting unfollow settled;
explicit synchronization before navigation repaired that particular flow.
This is evidence that authoritative settlement and replica freshness are
different boundaries, not advice to block every route on synchronization.

The [0.2.31 diary](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-20-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.31/astra/lane-diary.md)
successfully proved web offline reload and dual-offline convergence. It also
notes that offline search covers acquired history, and that broad public post
and aggregate replication is a scale tradeoff. Its
[report](../benchmarks/runs/2026-09-20-snapback4-clique-astra-0.2.31/astra/lane-final-report.md)
explicitly leaves native acquisition latency and memory unmeasured.

## 3. Current contract

The [README](../snapback4/packages/snapback4/README.md),
[sync card](../snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/sync.rs) and
[prediction card](../snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/prediction.rs)
already state these distinctions. `client.acquired`, `useAcquired()`,
`usePendingWrite`, `complete`, `fresh` and terminal write states exist. The
read card is not missing a new collection of application-specific states.

LLP 3000.001 owns replica lifecycle and retained-read composition. The
implemented LLP 3000.008 client increment is not undone by an application
route choosing the wrong prerequisite. Likewise, a missing native measurement
does not invalidate earlier reference-app measurements on their own fixtures.

## 4. Interpretation

The remaining friction is concentrated at the starter/application boundary.
The substrate supplies facts and durable behavior, but common examples can
still invite an online-first control flow. Each app then rediscovers shell
delivery, route resolution and how to display writes without predicted rows.

Replica placement is a separate deliberate choice: public does not mean that
every row must be acquired, and a small rendered page does not prove small
startup work. Choosing a horizon can also remove the negative knowledge a
mutation needs for prediction. Automatic blanket horizon insertion would
trade one surprise for another.

## 5. Desired outcome and confidence

A generated app should reopen held content without an online precondition,
preserve pending intent, render incomplete history honestly and make its
acquisition workload visible. It should leave the established client contract
and retention policy intact.

The route and shell observations are concrete application errors; whether a
better starter prevents them is testable. The latest diary establishes a
need to measure its residency choice, not a finding that current acquisition
is necessarily slow. The [child RFC](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md)
implements one supported Vite starter. The paired equivalent-residency
experiment is deferred; the [evidence child](./3000.011.005.000-qualify-the-built-application.rfc.md)
owns actual-app acquisition measurements, which do not substitute for that
two-configuration comparison.

## 6. Review disposition

Review scope correction: default init is schema-only, while Expo init copies a real application. The selected deliverable reuses the existing Vite chat reference and exposes that one output through init; it does not assume a web starter already exists.

    // Acknowledge the states actually observed before its file reads, never

    pub fn check_server_at(&self, baseline: &mut SourceStamp) -> Result<(), String> {
        let before = self.checked_dependencies();
        let checked = self.check_server();
        let observed = self.checked_dependencies();
        if observed != before { baseline.dependencies = stamp(observed); }
        checked

        let dir = self.generated_dir();

            std::fs::write(server, text)?;
        }

Native reads get 200,000,000 fuel units. Each instance
bounds its memory and table within the existing heap allowance; bridge buffers,
answers and logs also have byte bounds. A queued native job binds the exact wasm
bytes: changing or removing the module parks it with E_JOB_GENERATION before
another chunk can use its saved state. Legacy native runs without that binding
also park; already committed chunks remain committed. Identical bytes resume.
For a trap, fix the module and run `snapback4 check`; inspect the committed
chunks before queueing a replacement job. The failed chunk rolled back.
The module imports one host function and nothing else: no clock, no randomness,
no network — `chunk.now` is the host's — so a native job runs under the simulator
like any job. Quarry still declares
the table, its rules and its `sync`; a mutation cannot be native,
because a device predicts Quarry and the server replays it. Anything
that needs the network stays outside and writes into a table the job
reads. A query can be native too; a mutation cannot.

## Operations

`snapback/native/` is a Cargo package the app owns — `crate-type =
["cdylib"]`, its own `[workspace]`, a dependency on `snapback4-native` —
and `snapback4 check` writes `snapback/generated/native.rs` (a constant
per table, index and aggregate view; a `serde` struct per table), builds the
package for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` into `snapback/generated/native.wasm`
(cold about ten seconds, then a cargo no-op), and refuses if a declared
job is not one the module implements. `dev` rebuilds on every save under
`snapback/`; `deploy` ships the module with the release; `run <job>` runs
it here; `doctor` says whether the module and the toolchain are in place.

## Refusals

E_NATIVE means a native declaration or loaded module cannot perform the
requested operation. For example, `mutation rescore(): native` is invalid:
use `job rescore(): native` for chunked writes or `query rescore(): native`
for a read that never writes. Declare the matching function in the app's
`module!` registry and provide `snapback/native/Cargo.toml`. Run
`snapback4 check` to build and validate the module; the message lists missing
exports or load failures. A native query cannot call a write host function.

E_NATIVE_TOOLCHAIN means the native build cannot find Cargo or the
`wasm32-unknown-unknown` target. For example, a Rust install with only the
host target cannot build `snapback/native/`. Put Cargo on PATH and run
//! The filename is the declaration; helpers belong in subdirectories.
use crate::project::Project;
use std::{collections::BTreeMap, ffi::{CStr, CString, OsString}, fs::File, io, os::{fd::{AsRawFd, FromRawFd}, unix::ffi::{OsStrExt, OsStringExt}}, path::{Path, PathBuf}};

// Descriptor-relative opens pin each directory and refuse symlinks at every
// level. A concurrent rename cannot redirect a later copy through a pathname.
fn open(parent: &File, name: &std::ffi::OsStr) -> io::Result<File> {
    let name = CString::new(name.as_bytes())?;
    let fd = unsafe { libc::openat(parent.as_raw_fd(), name.as_ptr(), libc::O_RDONLY | libc::O_CLOEXEC | libc::O_NOFOLLOW | libc::O_NONBLOCK) };
    if fd < 0 { return Err(io::Error::last_os_error()); }
    Ok(unsafe { File::from_raw_fd(fd) })
}
fn copy_package(parent: &File, stage: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    let mut source = match open(parent, std::ffi::OsStr::new("package.json")) {
        Ok(file) => file,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(()),
        Err(error) => return Err(error),
    };
    if !source.metadata()?.is_file() { return Err(io::Error::other("worker package.json must be a regular file")); }
    let mut output = File::options().write(true).create_new(true).open(stage.join("package.json"))?;
    io::copy(&mut source, &mut output)?;
    Ok(())
}
fn entries(dir: &File) -> io::Result<Vec<OsString>> {
    if !dir.metadata()?.is_dir() { return Err(io::Error::other("worker source directory is not a directory")); }
    let fd = unsafe { libc::fcntl(dir.as_raw_fd(), libc::F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, 0) };
    if fd < 0 { return Err(io::Error::last_os_error()); }
    let stream = unsafe { libc::fdopendir(fd) };
    if stream.is_null() { unsafe { libc::close(fd); } return Err(io::Error::last_os_error()); }
    // readdir uses errno to distinguish EOF from an error.
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    let result = loop {
        #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
        unsafe { *libc::__errno_location() = 0; }
        #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
        unsafe { *libc::__error() = 0; }
        let entry = unsafe { libc::readdir(stream) };
        if entry.is_null() {
            let error = io::Error::last_os_error();
            break if error.raw_os_error() == Some(0) { Ok(names) } else { Err(error) };
        }
        let name = unsafe { CStr::from_ptr((*entry).d_name.as_ptr()) }.to_bytes();
        if name != b"." && name != b".." { names.push(OsString::from_vec(name.to_vec())); }
    };
    unsafe { libc::closedir(stream); }
    result
}
// Host-installed packages are separate from immutable source; restore every
// nested package scope that exists in the source snapshot.
fn link_package_scopes(source: &Path, target: &Path) -> io::Result<()> {
    if !target.is_dir() { return Ok(()); }
    let entries = match std::fs::read_dir(source) { Ok(entries) => entries, Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(()), Err(e) => return Err(e) };
    for entry in entries {
        let entry = entry?; let name = entry.file_name();
        if name == "node_modules" {
            if entry.path().is_dir() && !target.join(&name).exists() { std::os::unix::fs::symlink(entry.path(), target.join(&name))?; }
        } else if entry.file_type()?.is_dir() {
            link_package_scopes(&entry.path(), &target.join(name))?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn root(project: &Project) -> io::Result<Option<File>> {
    let project = File::open(project.root.canonicalize()?)?;
    let schema = match open(&project, std::ffi::OsStr::new("snapback")) {
        Ok(schema) => schema,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(None),
        Err(error) => return Err(error),
    };
    match open(&schema, std::ffi::OsStr::new("workers")) {
        Ok(dir) => Ok(Some(dir)),
        Err(error) if error.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(None),
        Err(error) => Err(error),
    }
}
fn walk(dir: &File, relative: &Path, dependencies: Option<(&Path, &Path)>, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&Path, &mut File) -> io::Result<()>) -> io::Result<()> {
    for name in entries(dir)? {
        // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — packages are host-owned, including nested
        // node_modules and package-manager symlinks. Never deploy their bytes
        // or validate them as source; launches link the original installation.
        if name == "node_modules" {
            if let Some((source, stage)) = dependencies {
                let target = stage.join(relative).join(&name);
                std::fs::create_dir_all(target.parent().unwrap())?;
                if !target.exists() { std::os::unix::fs::symlink(source.join(relative).join(&name), target)?; }
            }
            continue;
        }
        let mut file = open(dir, &name)?;
        let kind = file.metadata()?.file_type();
        let path = relative.join(name);
        if kind.is_dir() { walk(&file, &path, dependencies, visit)?; }
        else if kind.is_file() { visit(&path, &mut file)?; }
        else { return Err(io::Error::other("worker source must contain only directories and regular files (no symlinks)")); }
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.009#5.3 — one convention shared by development and immutable releases.
pub(super) fn declared(project: &Project) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, PathBuf>, String> {
    collect(project, None, false)
}

fn collect(project: &Project, stage: Option<&Path>, link_dependencies: bool) -> Result<BTreeMap<String, PathBuf>, String> {
    let mut workers = BTreeMap::new();
    let Some(dir) = root(project).map_err(|e| format!("workers: {e}"))? else { return Ok(workers); };
    let source_root = project.root.join("snapback/workers");
    let target_root = stage.map(|stage| stage.join("snapback/workers"));
    let dependencies = target_root.as_deref().filter(|_| link_dependencies).map(|target| (source_root.as_path(), target));

**NOT_READY**

One material concern:

- **§2 does not reconcile saved-source isolation with native/server companion generation.** Lines 54–59 promise deployment of the selected schema without generating its types, while taking companions from the current project. Those companions can depend on schema-specific generated files: the [native guide](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-proof/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/native.rs:104) requires generated Rust definitions, and the server handlers use generated signatures. If the working schema is A and the saved candidate is B, reusing A’s generated artifacts can produce incompatible companions; regenerating B’s artifacts in the working project violates isolation.

  **Required change:** define the companion preparation boundary explicitly. Generate and validate schema-dependent artifacts against B in a private staging tree, leaving the working schema and generated files untouched. If some companion configurations cannot support that path, refuse them before activation with an actionable diagnostic. Extend the already-planned saved-source isolation coverage to include a differing native schema and a server-handler schema, checking both staged consistency and unchanged working files.

The remaining proposed orchestration, identity checks, cleanup bounds, rendered-settlement ordering and four-attempt qualification requirements have no material design concerns from this review. Implementation qualification remains pending; it is not the reason for this verdict.
